# Changelog of the data structures

Changes to the tables, schemas and scripts (not to individual claims). Newest first.

## loc-12ag-maps: HQ 12th Army Group situation maps, pass 1 (31 October 2026)

Scott approved a pass over the Library of Congress series *HQ 12th Army Group situation maps* (public domain, 416 daily sheets 6 June 1944 to 26 July 1945) on 31 October 2026: one sheet a week at the 1200 hrs situation, in batches, one commit per batch, on branch `loc-12ag-maps` from `main` `62e97c3`. The maps are a separate source family (contemporary Allied situation maps): Allied units from their own reports and German units from G-2 estimates.

Rules applied (accepted by Scott): one reference for the series with the sheet id and situation date in each claim's `source_locator`; division points read as the main body and corps or army symbols as the headquarters at a precision of 10 km; German units at confidence 0.3; Allied units at 0.5, or 0.7 when another source family has the unit within 10 km on the same or the next day; no command relation and no parent from the maps (every new row has a blank parent); no front-line trace; units shown en route, marked as elements, unlocated or doubtful, and brigades and regiments stay in the private reading notes; placeholder units get no first claim; a unit shown between documented dates gets a claim; a disagreement with an existing row is logged as an open question and the existing row is unchanged. Commander is `unknown` on every new row because the maps name none. Reading notes are private.

### Registration data in the repository (1 November 2026)

- `tools/loc12ag/sheetG_registration.json` checked against the working transforms (27 weekly sheets, `cs000106`-`cs000289`, all identical); new `tools/loc12ag/pixel_to_latlon.py` reproduces every map coordinate from the sheet pixel; README updated. No data rows changed.

### Roetgen place and name-merge guard (1 November 2026)

- **New place** `PL2611` Roetgen, North Rhine-Westphalia (GeoNames `R782`, PPLA4 id 2844277; old value none, new value this row, reason: central Roetgen readings were named "Rott" by reverse geocoding and merged by name into `PL2002` Rott, about 4 km away).
- **Re-pointed** `C4880`, `C4988`, `C5103`, `C5216`, `C5337`, `C5455`, `C5588`, `C5717` (78th Infantry Division): place_id old value `PL2230` (Petergensfeld, 3.7 km), new value `PL2611` (Roetgen, 2.8-2.9 km); location_qualifier old 'near Petergensfeld', new 'near Roetgen'; reason: Roetgen is nearer the reading and was the true gazetteer candidate. Notes added to each row.
- **Tool** `tools/loc12ag/name_merge.py`: prefer the municipality name within 1.5 km of a GeoNames seat; refuse a name merge into an existing place more than 3 km away. `PL2002` unchanged.

### Tools (commit 1)

- **New helper folder** `tools/loc12ag/` (not part of the build or the checks): `sheet_index.csv` (416 sheets by date, no missing day; 50 weekly sheets flagged for pass 1), `georef.py` (graticule fit through a Lambert conformal conic plane and an affine transform with residuals in km; registration of other scans of the same base sheet by phase correlation) and `sheet3_control.json` (base sheet 3, Normandy, 14 graticule ticks, RMS about 1 km). Needs numpy and Pillow.

### Batch 1: Normandy, 6 June-25 July 1944 (8 weekly sheets)

- **New reference** `R781` (the map series; archive_document, family `loc_12ag_situation_maps`).
- **Sheets read:** cs000001 (6 June), cs000008 (13 June), cs000015 (20 June), cs000022 (27 June), cs000029 (4 July), cs000036 (11 July), cs000043 (18 July), cs000050 (25 July), all at 1200 hrs.
- **New places** `PL1143` to `PL1186` (44; OpenStreetMap Nominatim): Normandy communes within 3 km of a map point where no existing place was within 3 km.
- **New claims** `C2282` to `C2468` (187: 108 German at 0.3, 69 British and 10 Canadian; 10 Allied rows at 0.7 and 69 at 0.5; each note carries old value none, new value and reason). Event type `other` for division points and `hq_observation` for corps and army headquarters; `location_basis` documented_on_date; each row keeps its own map point in `coord_override` within 3 km of its place.
  - Parent blank on all 187 (inferred value: old none, new blank, reason: no command relation is taken from the situation maps).
  - Commander `unknown` on all 187 (old none, new unknown, reason: the maps name no commander).
- **Agreement with the dataset:** 73 new rows have an existing row of the same unit within three days; 42 of them are within 10 km (58%). Most of the 31 others compare the map's main-body point with an objective or front-line point 10-20 km away; the larger differences are open points in `docs/roadmap.md`.
- **Not written:** seven readings of placeholder units (265th, 266th and 276th Infantry Divisions), three readings with no gazetteer place within 3 km, and every symbol marked elements, unlocated, doubtful or en route (private notes).
- **No existing row changed.** No relation, no conflict group (next `CR1067`, `G034`).
- **Semantic scenario 53.**

### Batch 2us: US units on the eight Normandy sheets, 6 June-25 July 1944

Scott on 31 October 2026 asked for US units on every sheet, not only gaps, to measure the maps against the dense US data and to give US units a second source family.

- **Rule added (accepted by Scott):** US rows at 0.5, or 0.7 when another source family has the unit within 10 km and within three days (old window for Allied rows: same or next day; reason: Scott's instruction for US units; British, Canadian and German rows keep the batch 1 rule). US division symbols are read as the main body at the box centre; corps, army and army group headquarters flags are read at the foot of the flag staff (the dot), where the sheet draws one. A division drawn twice (a boxed command post on a staff behind a box at the front, as on the 13 June sheet) is read only at the unstaffed box; the staffed division command posts are not written in this batch (private notes).
- **New claims** `C2469` to `C2582` (114 US: 92 division rows, 22 corps, army and army group headquarters; 28 at 0.7, 86 at 0.5). Parent blank on all (old none, new blank, reason: no command relation is taken from the maps); commander `unknown` on all (old none, new unknown, reason: the maps name no commander). First rows for the 12th Army Group headquarters on the maps (`US-AG-012G`, 25 July).
- **New places** `PL1187` to `PL1220` (34; OpenStreetMap Nominatim, communes within 3 km of a map point).
- **Agreement:** 45 new US rows have an existing row of another source family within three days; 28 are within 10 km (62%). Division rows 28 of 37 (76%); corps and army headquarters 0 of 8, all against objective or sector-centre rows 14-54 km away. Against existing headquarters or main-body rows only: 14 of 19 (74%).
- **Gaps:** 75 new rows have no existing row of the unit within two days, 49 none within six days.
- **Not written:** 9 readings with no gazetteer place within 3 km, 1 duplicate (35th Infantry Division, 11 July, same date and event type already present), the dashed XV Corps box of 18 July (en route).
- **`data/coordinate_exceptions.csv`:** one speed exception added, `C0214`/`C2528` (82nd Airborne Division, Normandy on 11 July, England on 13 July: a sea move after relief); old value none, new value the exception, reason: the 370 km in two days is the Channel crossing. `C2528` says so in its note; `C0214` is unchanged.
- **`scripts/semantic_tests.py`:** scenario 53 expectation of (echelon, location_represents) pairs, old value division, corps and army only, new value with `('army_group', 'headquarters')`, reason: batch 2us adds the 12th Army Group headquarters under R781. New scenario 54.
- **No existing row changed.** No relation, no conflict group.

### Batch 3a: August 1944, weekly sheets of 1, 8, 15 and 22 August (all nationalities)

- **Sheets read:** cs000057 (1 August, base sheet 3), cs000064 (8 August), cs000071 (15 August), cs000078 (22 August), all at 1200 hrs. The last three are new base sheets; each was fitted from 9 to 13 town junctions (`tools/loc12ag/sheetB_control.json` to `sheetD_control.json`, RMS about 1 km, leave-one-out at most 3.7 km).
- **New claims** `C2583` to `C2809` (227: 72 US, 81 British, Canadian, Polish and French, 74 German; 34 at 0.7, 119 at 0.5, 74 at 0.3), first map rows for the French 2nd Armoured Division and the 1st Polish Armoured Division. Same rules as batches 1 and 2us; parent blank and commander unknown on all (old none, new blank or unknown, reasons as in batch 1).
- **New places** `PL1221` to `PL1346` (126; OpenStreetMap Nominatim, communes within 3 km of a map point, now also in Brittany, Maine, Perche and Île-de-France).
- **Agreement (existing row of the unit within three days, within 10 km):** US 21 of 53 (40%; within one day 11 of 30, 37%), British, Canadian, Polish and French 19 of 37 (51%), German 2 of 8. Lower than in Normandy because units moved 30-200 km in three days during the breakout and pursuit (22 August against rows of 25 August on the Seine); see the roadmap.
- **Gaps:** 146 new rows have no existing row of the unit within two days (US 32, others 48, German 66).
- **Not written:** 30 readings with no place within 3 km, 8 placeholder readings (265th, 266th, 276th, 326th, 343rd and 348th Infantry, 16th and 17th Luftwaffe Field Divisions), 1 duplicate, symbols marked destroyed or unlocated (the Loire group of 8 August), en route boxes, combat commands.
- **`data/coordinate_exceptions.csv`:** two speed exceptions added, `C1608`/`C2653` and `C1611`/`C2766` (French 2nd Armoured Division: 8 August assembly south of Avranches to Le Mans on 9 August, and Argentan on 22 August to Rambouillet on 23 August); old value none, new value the exception, reason: documented road marches of 130 and 144 km. `C2653` and `C2766` say so in their notes; `C1608` and `C1611` are unchanged.
- **New scenario 55.** No existing row changed. No relation, no conflict group.

### Village places for readings with no town within 3 km (batches 1, 2us and 3a)

Scott on 31 October 2026 approved adding village-level places for the 42 map readings that batches 1, 2us and 3a had skipped because no gazetteer town lay within 3 km.
- **New places** `PL1347` to `PL1384` (38; OpenStreetMap Nominatim hamlet and village points within 3 km of the map point, searched 31 October 2026). Names are `Hamlet (commune), Department` or `Village, Department`. Where OpenStreetMap gives a post-1944 merged commune, the 1944 commune is used: for villages that were 1944 communes (Sermentot, Étouvy, Coulonces, Saint-Martin-de-Tallevende, Champ-du-Boult, La Cochère, Le Roncenay-Authenay, Crennes) the note names the modern commune; for seven hamlets (`La Flague`, `La Marvindière`, `La Morlière de Bas`, `La Haute Barre`, `Les Moulins`, `Radray`, `La Saulnerie`) the 1944 commune is inferred from the nearest village centre and marked "to be confirmed"; two hamlets keep the modern commune (`Le Neufbourg (Le Molay-Littry)`, `La Sauvagère (Valdallière)`) and are listed for renaming in the roadmap. `La Fontaine Pesée (Étampes)` uses the 1944 department Seine-et-Oise (Essonne today).
- **New claims** `C2810` to `C2850` (41: 26 US, 5 British, 10 German; 7 at 0.7, 24 at 0.5, 10 at 0.3), written exactly as the other map rows (same confidence rules, representative point, precision 10 km, parent blank and commander unknown on all: old value none, new value blank or unknown, reason: no command relation or commander is taken from the situation maps). Two places are shared: `PL1350` La Madeleine (Sainte-Marie-du-Mont) for the 4th Infantry Division on 6 June and the 5th Infantry Division on 11 July, `PL1352` and `PL1354` for the two readings each of the 101st Airborne Division and V Corps.
- **Skipped (1):** the 338th Infantry Division on 22 August 1944 (sheet cs000078, 48.3655 N 2.7552 E, Forêt de Fontainebleau): the nearest locality is a forest hamlet 2.9 km away and its 1944 commune is not resolved, so the reading stays ambiguous.
- **Agreement (existing row of the unit within three days, within 10 km):** 8 of 18 comparable rows (44%): US 7 of 15, British 1 of 1, German 0 of 2. **Gaps:** 27 new rows have no existing row of the unit within two days.
- **`C0383` investigated, not changed** (9th Infantry Division command post, 8 August 1944, `PL0355` La Tuilerie, Calvados, 49.219 N 0.790 W): see the roadmap.
- **New scenario 56.** No existing claim or place row changed. No relation, no conflict group.

### Place fix: `C0383` (9th Infantry Division command post, 8 August 1944)

Scott on 31 October 2026 approved re-pointing `C0383` (roadmap point 24).
- **`C0383`:** `place_id` old value `PL0355` (La Tuilerie, Calvados, 49.2190 N 0.7900 W, at Le Tronquay near Bayeux), new value `PL1385`; latitude and longitude old value 49.2190, -0.7900, new value 48.8299, -1.1282; `location_basis` old value `town_centroid`, new value `inferred`. Reason: the old point is about 45 km north of the division, behind the British sector. Evidence: CMH ETO-OB (`R372`) lists the command posts St-Aubin-des-Bois, Calvados (5 August), "La Tuilerie (1 mi SE)", Calvados (8 August) and Forien, Manche (9 August, Saint-Laurent-de-Cuves); the 12th Army Group map of 8 August (`C2651`, `R781`) puts the division near Saint-Michel-de-Montjoie, 10 km from the new point. The exact La Tuilerie was not found: no such name is in OpenStreetMap or in the IGN gazetteer (lieux-dits and addresses) in the 56 communes around Saint-Aubin-des-Bois, Saint-Michel-de-Montjoie and Saint-Laurent-de-Cuves (searched 31 October 2026). The new point is a representative point at Saint-Aubin-des-Bois, the last named command post and the only Calvados commune on the route, precision 10 km. Confidence unchanged (0.5).
- **New place `PL1385`** (La Tuilerie (not located), near Saint-Aubin-des-Bois, Calvados; OpenStreetMap Nominatim point of Saint-Aubin-des-Bois). `PL0355` is unchanged and still used by `C2621` (21st Army Group, 1 August, near Le Tronquay).
- **Scenario 56** now expects `C0383` on `PL1385` (old expectation: unchanged on `PL0355`).

### Batch 3b: weekly sheets 29 August to 19 September 1944 (all nationalities)

Scott on 31 October 2026 asked to continue the weekly sheets from 29 August with all nationalities, US included.
- **Sheets:** `cs000085` (29 August), `cs000092` (5 September), `cs000099` (12 September), `cs000106` (19 September). Three new base sheets fitted from town junctions (`tools/loc12ag/sheetE_control.json`, `sheetF_control.json`, `sheetG_control.json`; RMS 1.3-1.7 km, leave-one-out at most 5.2 km); the 19 September sheet is registered to the 12 September scan (`sheetG_registration.json`). The claim note of every row gives the number of towns and the fit figures of its sheet.
- **New claims** `C2851` to `C3081` (231: 82 US, 76 British, Canadian, Polish, Belgian and French, 73 German; 17 at 0.7, 141 at 0.5, 73 at 0.3), written as the earlier map rows (representative point, precision 10 km, parent blank and commander unknown on all: old value none, new value blank or unknown, reason: no command relation or commander is taken from the situation maps). 118 fill gaps between documented dates (no existing row of the unit within three days, rows before and after).
- **New places** `PL1386` to `PL1581` (196; OpenStreetMap Nominatim town and village points within 3 km of the map point, searched 31 October 2026), including the first map places in Belgium, the Netherlands, Luxembourg and Germany; names follow `Town, Province` (Belgium), `Town, Province` (Netherlands), `Town, Land` (Germany) and `Village (canton), Luxembourg`.
- **Not written:** 34 readings with no town within 3 km (for a later village pass), 13 readings of placeholder units (Luftwaffe field divisions 17 and 18, the 47th, 331st and 348th Infantry Divisions), 1 duplicate, the units stacked at the map edges with arrows (off-map positions: VIII Corps in Brittany, the Le Havre and Channel-port garrisons, Third Army units at the east edge on 5 September), schematic boxes and arrows (Channel ports, Walcheren, the Seventh Army line of 12 September), elements (the 48th Infantry Division "ELMS" on 12 September) and German divisions whose dataset unit starts later (16th, 19th, 36th, 553rd and 559th Volksgrenadier, 176th and 462nd z.b.V. before October 1944). All are in the private notes.
- **Agreement (existing non-map row of the unit within 10 km):** one-day window 13 of 42 (31%): US 9 of 27 (33%), British and other Allied 4 of 11 (36%), German 0 of 4; three-day window 22 of 95 (23%): US 13 of 51 (25%), British and other Allied 9 of 24 (38%), German 0 of 20. Most one-day differences are 10-25 km between the map's front-line division symbol and a command post or objective row; within 20 km the US one-day figure is 18 of 27 (67%).
- **Coordinate checks:** nine new speed warnings (non-failing) pair a map row with an existing objective, front-line or sector-centre row 1-2 days away; no exception added (roadmap).
- **New scenario 57;** scenario 53 now also accepts brigade rows as `main_body` (old expectation: divisions, corps, armies and army groups only), because batch 3b adds Panzer brigades and the Belgian brigade. No existing claim row changed. No relation, no conflict group.

### Village pass 2 (batch 3b readings), the renaming list and the 12th Army Group headquarters move

Scott on 31 October 2026 accepted the recommendations of roadmap points 26 to 31: a village pass for batch 3b, the modern and French-language names on the renaming list, and the 12th Army Group headquarters rows moved to the town.
- **New places** `PL1582` to `PL1607` (26; OpenStreetMap Nominatim village, hamlet, suburb and locality points within 3 km of the map point, searched 31 October 2026, in France, Belgium, the Netherlands and Germany). Names are `Hamlet (commune), Department or province` or `Village, Department or province`. Belgian, Dutch and German hamlets carry the present section or village name and are marked "1944 municipality to be confirmed"; Artonges and Coudekerque name the modern merged commune in the note.
- **New claims** `C3082` to `C3107` (26: 8 US, 7 British, Canadian and Belgian, 11 German; 2 at 0.7, 13 at 0.5, 11 at 0.3), written as the other map rows (parent blank and commander unknown on all: old value none, new value blank or unknown, reason: no command relation or commander is taken from the situation maps).
- **Not written (8):** 6 with no named settlement within 3 km, 1 whose only named point is a wood, 1 duplicate (28th Infantry Division, 29 August: `C0399` has the same date and event type). See roadmap point 32.
- **Agreement (existing row within three days, within 10 km):** 2 of 10 (US 2 of 5, British 0 of 1, German 0 of 4). **Gaps:** 3 new rows fall between documented dates with no existing row within three days.
- **`C3018` (12th Army Group, 12 September 1944):** place_id old value `PL1535` (Nanterre), new value `PL0364` (Versailles); latitude/longitude old value 48.8958/2.1910 (box centre), new value 48.8053/2.1272; reason: the headquarters is the town at the flag, not the label box (roadmap point 31, approved by Scott). **`C3070` (19 September 1944):** place_id old value `PL1572` (Ornes), new value `PL0361` (Verdun); latitude/longitude old value 49.2475/5.4359, new value 49.1620/5.3876; same reason. Both rows now cite the coordinate reference of the town place (`R002`, `R001`); `coord_override` old value the box-centre text, new value blank (the claim point is the town point; the old point is in the row note). The places `PL1535` (Nanterre) and `PL1572` (Ornes), added in batch 3b for these two rows only, are removed as unused (ids retired, never on `main`).
- **Renaming list** extended (roadmap point 33). No place renamed yet.
- **`scripts/semantic_tests.py`:** scenario 53 expectation "every row keeps its own map point", old value all R781 rows, new value all R781 rows except `C3018` and `C3070`, reason: those two now sit on the town point by Scott's decision (point 31).
- **New scenario 58.** No other existing row changed. No relation, no conflict group.

### Rule of 31 October 2026: map rows are coarse filler

Scott on 31 October 2026 decided that the situation-map rows are coarse filler and never outrank a text row: from now on a map row is set to 0.7 (two source families) only when an existing non-map row of the unit agrees **within 5 km** (old value: within 10 km) on the same day (US: within three days). Applies to new rows from village pass 3 on; earlier rows are not changed. `tools/loc12ag` generation and the `R781` reliability note now say so (reliability_note old value "0.7 when another family agrees within 10 km on the same or the next day", new value adds the 5 km rule and its date). 54 of the 106 earlier map rows at 0.7 rest on agreement of 5-10 km (roadmap point 40, for Scott's decision).

### Village pass 5: GeoNames for the radius search (readings of 14 November 1944)

The OpenStreetMap Overpass service (main server and two mirrors) was unreachable from 31 October to 1 November 2026, so Scott on 1 November 2026 asked to switch the "named place within 3 km" search to an offline GeoNames gazetteer.
- **Method:** the free GeoNames country dumps NL, BE, LU, DE and FR (download.geonames.org/export/dump/, downloaded 1 November 2026, kept on the box only, never committed); populated places (feature class P: PPL, PPLL, PPLX, PPLF, PPLS, PPLA-PPLA4, PPLC, PPLG; historical and abandoned places excluded) searched locally within 3 km of the map point; nearest village preferred within 1 km of the nearest place. Nominatim (1.1 s between requests, retries with backoff) is used only to name the municipality and region. New reference **`R782`** (GeoNames, CC BY 4.0) is the coordinate reference of the places found this way.
- **New claims** `C4122` to `C4127` (6: 10th SS Panzer at Hoekelum, 51st Division at Boshoven, XXX Corps at Schootshei, 43rd Division at Heiwik, 361st at La Hoube, 16th at Hirtengaerten), written as the other rows of the sheet (0.3 German, 0.5 others; parent blank, commander unknown: old value none, new value blank or unknown, reason: no command relation or commander is taken from the situation maps).
- **New places** `PL2206` to `PL2211` (GeoNames points, local names).
- **Still unplaced:** the 85th Infantry Division (point on the water of the Haringvliet, no populated place within 3 km).
- No existing claim row changed.

## fms-german-6: two more FMS manuscripts (B-745, B-151; 31 October 2026)

Scott approved reading B-745 on Fold3 and B-151 from the Internet Archive and one data branch for the two on 31 October 2026, one commit per manuscript. Branch `fms-german-6` from `main` `1082707`. B-745 is read in the German original. B-151 is read in the US Army English translation from the Internet Archive because neither B-151 nor its sequel B-151a is on the Fold3 M1035 fiche and the German original is held only at the Bundesarchiv; the item was catalogued as B-151a but every page is B-151. Both are US Government works; the claims paraphrase them in English. The reading notes stay private and are not in this repository.

Rules applied (accepted by Scott): no command relation and no parent from a single manuscript (every new row has a blank parent); no Allied dates from the manuscripts; strengths stay in the reading notes; no front-line traces; confidence 0.5 for one source, 0.7 when a second source family agrees, 0.3 for arrivals, approximate days, doubtful readings and map positions; disagreements with existing rows stay open points and no existing row is changed. No first claim is given to a placeholder unit (the 257th Volksgrenadier and 106th Infantry Divisions and Divisions Nr. 405 and 905 stay placeholders).

### B-745 (Brandenberger, Nineteenth Army, 1 April-5 May 1945, German original)

- **New reference** `R779` (B-745; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). Reliability: 8 of 12 checkable facts agree with the dataset (67%).
- **New places** `PL1112` to `PL1137` (26; OpenStreetMap Nominatim): the Nineteenth Army command posts of Anlage 4 from Dettenbach to Pfunds and the corps and division command posts of 1 and 9 April.
- **New claims** `C2248` to `C2274` (27; 2 at 0.3 and the rest at 0.5; each note carries old value none, new value and reason): the Nineteenth Army command post at Dettenbach on 1 April and its eleven moves to Jungingen, Mühlingen, Ravensburg, Schlier, Kempten, Burgberg, Lermoos, Ehrwald, Karrösten, Pians and Pfunds; the LXIV Corps at Ödsbach (1 April) and Enzklösterle (9 April) and near Landeck on 5 May (0.3, sketch); the XVIII SS Corps at St. Trudpert (1 April) and Schonach (21 April); the LXXX Corps at Neckarrems (9 April) and Münsingen (22 April, 0.3, sketch); the Twenty-fourth Army at Engen (1 April) and Eglofs (26 April); the 716th Division at Waldrennach, the 559th at Mundelsheim, the 198th at Kleinbottwar and the 47th at Monrepos; the 16th Volksgrenadier Division assembled south of Gültlingen (18 April); the 189th Division attack from Laichingen (22 April).
  - Parent blank on all 27 (inferred value: old none, new blank, reason: no command relation from a single manuscript).
- **No existing row changed.** No relation, no conflict group (next `CR1067`, `G034`).
- **Coordinate exceptions:** two speed exceptions added for `C1217` (a Lexikon table row with a representative point at Donaueschingen on 26 April) against the new command posts `C2254` (Kempten) and `C2255` (Burgberg); old value none, new value an explained exception, reason: the table row is not a headquarters position.
- **Disagreements recorded, no value changed:** Brandenberger taking command on 1 April (arrived 28 March) in B-745 against 26 March in `C1190`; the LXXX Corps under the Nineteenth Army from 9 April in B-745 against 8 April in `C2123`; the XVIII SS Corps staff dissolved at 2200 on 26 April in B-745 against 27 April in `C1273`; the Twenty-fourth Army shipping its troops across Lake Constance in the night to 26 April in B-745 against 21 April in `C1219`.
- **Semantic scenario 51.**

### B-151 (Manteuffel, Fifth Panzer Army, September-16 December 1944, English translation from the Internet Archive)

- **New reference** `R780` (B-151; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). Reliability: 6 of 8 checkable facts agree with the dataset (75%).
- **New places** `PL1138` to `PL1142` (5; OpenStreetMap Nominatim): Le Hohwald, Manderscheid in the Bernkastel-Wittlich district, Dackscheid, Kyllburg, Gerolstein.
- **New claims** `C2275` to `C2281` (7; 3 at 0.3 and the rest at 0.5; each note carries old value none, new value and reason): Fifth Panzer Army headquarters at Le Hohwald on Manteuffel's arrival (11 September), at Königshoven on the Aachen front (22 October), the operations staff to Manderscheid (23 November) and the forward move to Dackscheid (15 December); the LVIII Panzer Corps at Eisenschmitt (23 November, 0.3, approximate day); the XLVII Panzer Corps staff complete at Kyllburg (8 December, 0.3, arrival); the LXVI Corps headquarters at Gerolstein (4 November, 0.3, corps number printed as 65).
  - Parent blank on all 7 (inferred value: old none, new blank, reason: no command relation from a single manuscript).
- **New place rather than reuse:** `PL1066` (Manderscheid, used by `C2179`) has the coordinates of the Manderscheid near Arzfeld in the western Eifel; B-151 places the army staff near Eisenschmitt and Gerolstein, which fits Manderscheid in the Bernkastel-Wittlich district, so the new row uses the new place `PL1139` and `PL1066` and `C2179` are unchanged (open point).
- **No existing row changed.** No relation, no conflict group (next `CR1067`, `G034`).
- **Disagreements recorded, no value changed:** the army staff leaving the Aachen sector on 23 November in B-151 against 15 November in `C0956` and 30 November in `C2179`.
- **Semantic scenario 52.**

## fms-german-5: four more FMS manuscripts (B-781, B-831, B-084, B-849; 31 October 2026)

Scott approved reading four more Foreign Military Studies manuscripts on Fold3 and one data branch for all four on 31 October 2026, one commit per manuscript. Branch `fms-german-5` from `main` `67a7d83`. The German original is the primary source in B-781, B-084 and B-849, with the English translation used only to check unclear passages; in B-831 the German scan is illegible, so the English translation is the primary text and the reference says so. All four are US Government works; the claims paraphrase them in English. The reading notes stay private and are not in this repository.

Rules applied (accepted by Scott): no command relation and no parent from a single manuscript (every new row has a blank parent); no Allied dates from the manuscripts; strengths stay in the reading notes; no front-line traces; confidence 0.5 for one source, 0.7 when a second source family agrees, 0.3 for arrivals, approximate days, doubtful places and map positions; disagreements with existing rows are recorded as open points and the existing rows are left unchanged.

### B-781 (Wiese, Nineteenth Army, mid-September-18 December 1944, German original)

- **New reference** `R775` (B-781; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). Reliability: 17 of 19 checkable facts agree with the dataset (89%).
- **New places** `PL1083` to `PL1084` (2; OpenStreetMap Nominatim): Le Thillot, Hirtzfelden.
- **New claims** `C2209` to `C2213` (5; all at 0.3; each note carries old value none, new value and reason): the LXVI Corps at Bains-les-Bains, the LXIV Corps at Remiremont, the IV Luftwaffe Field Corps at Le Thillot and the LXXXV Corps at Belfort in mid-September (all 0.3, approximate day); the LXIII Corps flag at Hirtzfelden on the sketch of 6 December (0.3).
  - Parent blank on all 5 (inferred value: old none, new blank, reason: no command relation from a single manuscript).
- **No existing row changed.** No relation, no conflict group (next `CR1067`, `G034`).
- **Disagreements recorded, no value changed:** the LXIII Corps formed just before the attack of 14 November in B-781 against the Dehner organization becoming the LXIII Corps on 18 November in `C1023`; Wiese handing over on 18 December in B-781 (agrees with `C2113`) against 15 December in `C1018`.
- **Semantic scenario 47.**

### B-831 (Felber, Seventh Army, 20 February-26 March 1945, English translation as primary text because the German scan is illegible)

- **New reference** `R776` (B-831; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). Reliability: 9 of 9 checkable facts agree with the dataset (100%).
- **New places** `PL1085` to `PL1096` (12; OpenStreetMap Nominatim): Eisenschmitt, Dierfeld, Krastel, Nieder-Olm, Kirchheimbolanden, Bensheim, Heigenbrücken, Wißmannsdorf, Salm, Pohlbach, Schifferstadt, Boppard.
- **New claims** `C2214` to `C2225` (12; 1 at 0.3 and the rest at 0.5; each note carries old value none, new value and reason): Seventh Army command posts at Eisenschmitt (21 February), Dierfeld (4 March), Krastel (10 March), Nieder-Olm (16 March), Kirchheimbolanden (17 March), Bensheim (21 March) and Heigenbrücken (26 March); LIII Corps at Wißmannsdorf (20 February) and Salm (2 March, 0.3, place uncertain); LXXX Corps at Pohlbach (5 March) and Schifferstadt (21 March); LXXXIX Corps at Boppard (15 March).

- **No existing row changed.** No relation, no conflict group (next `CR1067`, `G034`).
- **Disagreements recorded, no value changed:** none; the LXXXIX Corps command post dated from 10 March in B-831 is a day after its arrival at the Moselle on 9 March in `C1175`, read as consistent.
- **Semantic scenario 48.**

### B-084 (Schlemm, First Parachute Army, February-10 March 1945, German original)

- **New reference** `R777` (B-084; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). Reliability: 6 of 8 checkable facts agree with the dataset (75%).
- **New places** `PL1097` to `PL1100` (4; OpenStreetMap Nominatim): Saalhoff, Marienbaum, Sonsbeck, Birten.
- **New claims** `C2226` to `C2235` (10; 1 at 0.3 and the rest at 0.5; each note carries old value none, new value and reason): First Parachute Army command posts west of Xanten (8 February), at Saalhoff (22 February) and west of Wesel (2 March) and the end of the Wesel bridgehead (10 March); XLVII Panzer Corps sectors at Marienbaum (24 February) and Xanten-Sonsbeck (4 March); II Parachute Corps sectors at Udem-Weeze (24 February) and Sonsbeck-Issum (4 March) and its command of the last bridgehead (7 March); the 8th Parachute Division brought north from Roermond (16 February, 0.3).

### B-849 (von Zangen, Fifteenth Army in the Ruhr pocket, 31 March-15 April 1945, German original)

- **New reference** `R778` (B-849; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). Reliability: 9 of 11 checkable facts agree with the dataset (82%).

## fms-german-4: four German FMS manuscripts read in the original (B-475, B-235, B-179, B-763; 31 October 2026)

Scott approved reading four German Foreign Military Studies manuscripts on Fold3 and one data branch for all four on 31 October 2026, one commit per manuscript. Branch `fms-german-4` from `main` `81e0aec`. The German original is the primary source in each; the English translation (B-179 and B-763 only) was used to check unclear passages. All four are US Government works; the claims paraphrase the German text in English. The reading notes stay private and are not in this repository.

Rules applied (accepted by Scott): no command relation and no parent from a single manuscript (every new row has a blank parent); no Allied dates from the manuscripts; strengths stay in the reading notes; no front-line traces; confidence 0.5 for one source, 0.7 when a second source family agrees, 0.3 for arrivals, approximate days and map positions.

### B-475 (von Zangen, Fifteenth Army, 15 September-10 November 1944)

- **New reference** `R771` (B-475, von Zangen, 1947; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). Reliability: 10 of 14 checkable facts agree with the dataset (71%).
- **New places** `PL1057` to `PL1065` (9; OpenStreetMap Nominatim): Moergestel, Dongen, Noordeloos, Geldermalsen, Rijkevorsel, Merksem, Schijndel, Roosendaal and Dordrecht.
- **New claims** `C2164` to `C2178` (15; each note carries old value none, new value and reason): LXVII Corps command posts at Esschen (23 September) and Breda-South (from 25 September); LXXXVIII Corps at Moergestel (23 September), Dongen (about 24 September, 0.3) and Noordeloos (from 8 November); the LXXXIX Corps staff arriving at Geldermalsen (26/27 September, 0.3); the 712th Division reaching Tilburg (24 September, 0.3); the 711th and 346th relief north of Antwerp (26/27 September, 0.3); the 59th taking the Schijndel sector (28 September); Kampfgruppe Chill arriving south of Tilburg (4 October, 0.3); the 719th taking over south of Tilburg (10/11 October); the 256th VG relieving the 245th near Tilburg and the 245th moving to Roosendaal (23 October, 0.3); the air attack on the Fifteenth Army command post at Dordrecht (about 26 October, 0.3).

- **Disagreements recorded, no value changed:** the 245th Division in line at Bladel-Hapert-Wintelre by 16 September in B-475 against South Beveland on 17 September in `C0893`; the 59th Division at Schijndel-Boxtel in October against the Lexikon row at Arnhem (`C0921`); the LXXXVIII Corps north of the Maas from 6 November against Tilburg on 10 November in `C1044`; the German forces at Arnhem directly under Army Group B (B-475, hedged) against the First Parachute Army from 23 September in `C0855`.

### B-235 (Wagener, Fifth Panzer Army in the Ardennes, November 1944-January 1945)

- **New reference** `R772` (B-235, Wagener, 1945; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). Reliability: 17 of 19 checkable facts agree with the dataset (89%).

Not used: the fall of St. Vith (22 December in B-235; an Allied date) and the arrival list of the other reinforcements (in the reading notes).

### B-179 (Hausser, Seventh Army 25 July-20 August 1944)

- **New reference** `R773` (B-179, Hausser, 1946; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). The German text (images 77-202) is primary; the English translation (images 2-75) was used only as a check. Reliability: 22 of 24 checkable facts agree with the dataset (92%).

- **Semantic scenario 45.** Scenarios 43 and 44: the check that confidence is 0.5 or 0.3 now compares sets (old value: a list comparison that could pass wrongly; new value: a subset test; reason: the scenario 45 run exposed it).

### B-763 (Pemsel, Seventh Army 6 June-29 July 1944)

- **New reference** `R774` (B-763, Pemsel, 1948; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). The German text and its 29 Skizzen (images 1-251) are primary; the English translation (images 252-453) was used only to check unclear passages. Reliability: 17 of 20 checkable facts agree with the dataset (85%).
- **New places** `PL1079` to `PL1082` (4; OpenStreetMap Nominatim): Pontécoulant, Presles, Fervaches and Ouville.
- **New claims** `C2203` to `C2208` (6, all 0.3 as sketch positions; each note carries old value none, new value and reason): headquarters flags of the XLVII Panzer Corps at Pontécoulant (13-15 June), the Seventh Army forward command post at Presles (16-18 June) and at Le Laurier south-west of Percy (24-26 July), the II Parachute Corps at Fervaches (16-18 June), and the LXXXIV Corps at Saint-Sauveur-Lendelin (13-16 July) and Ouville (24-26 July).
  - Parent blank on all 6 (inferred value: old none, new blank, reason: no command relation from a single manuscript).
- **Changed row:** `C2193` (B-179 batch, Seventh Army forward command post north-east of Avranches, 28 July): `reference_ids` old `R773;R001`, new `R773;R774;R001`; `source` and `source_locator` gain B-763 p. 108; confidence stays 0.5 because both are one source family.
- **Disagreements recorded, no value changed:** the LXXXIV Corps headquarters at Périers on the sketch of 13-15 June against Saint-Lô in `C0106`, `C0116`, `C0123` and `C0128`; Dollmann's death on 28 June in B-763 against the alternate 29 June in `C0204` (agrees with `C0188`); the 1st SS Panzer Division near Thury-Harcourt and Clinchamps on 27-28 June on the sketches against its arrival at Bretteville on 6 July in `C0772`.
- **Semantic scenario 46.**

### Roadmap: FMS translation task

- `docs/roadmap.md` gains a future task: exact translations of scanned public-domain FMS manuscripts with maps and figures from free CARL or NARA copies, not Fold3. No data changed.

## official-history-pass-2: US gaps, a German refit span and the pilot-division labels (31 October 2026)

Scott approved official-history pass 2 on 31 October 2026, as one branch with one commit per source. Branch `official-history-pass-2` from `main` `13be4a4`.

### Chunk 1, US gaps (The Last Offensive)

- Source: the US Army official history (CMH), MacDonald, The Last Offensive (`R568` chapter 18 and `R569` chapter 19, HyperWar text); paraphrase with printed pages. No new reference.
- New place: `PL1055` Berchtesgaden, Bayern (OpenStreetMap Nominatim).
- 1 new claim: C2160, 101st Airborne, 8 May 1945, Berchtesgaden: garrison point (inferred), 0.3. Old value of each activity column: none (the row is new). Reason: the division raced for Berchtesgaden on 4 May (p. 442) and was arranging an SS surrender on 7 May (p. 473); no place is given for 8 May, so the last named objective is used and the state is inferred.
- Semantic tests: new scenario 42; scenario 34 and the batch filter (`NOT_OHP`) now leave out pass 2 rows (`OHP2`). Old filter: rows whose notes name the official-history pass. New filter: the same less `C2160`. Reason: the pass 2 notes also name the official-history pass, and the pass 1 totals stay as approved.
- Still open (no dated source in The Last Offensive): 8 May rows for the 70th and 106th; the open spans of the 11th Armored (23 December 1944), 26th (29 January 1945), 70th (17 February 1945), 104th (23 February 1945) and 106th (15 March 1945); the undated rests listed in the roadmap.

### Chunk 2, German refit spans (Lexikon der Wehrmacht)

- Source: Lexikon der Wehrmacht, 9. Panzer-Division (`R610`); short dated facts only, paraphrased. Pages read: 9th, 2nd, 21st and 116th Panzer and the 226th Infantry; only the 9th Panzer page dates a late-1944 refit (the 116th Panzer refit in the Eifel has no date).
- New place: `PL1056` Rheydt, Mönchengladbach (OpenStreetMap Nominatim).
- 3 new claims, `C2161` to `C2163` (old value of each activity column: none, the rows are new):
  - C2161, 9th Panzer, 27 October 1944, Venlo: in_line start (stated), 0.3. Relief attack for the 15th Army in the Venlo-Sittard area (27 to 30 October); representative point.
  - C2162, 9th Panzer, 31 October 1944, Rheydt: refitting start (stated, derived start day), 0.3. Short refit in the Rheydt area after the attack.
  - C2163, 9th Panzer, 15 November 1944, Geilenkirchen: in_line start (stated), 0.5. Defensive fighting for Geilenkirchen from 15 November; ends the refit span by the implicit-end rule.
- Not changed: the 226th Division (Dünkirchen) stays blank. Its only row is a month-level listing, and scenario 32 keeps listings unlabelled.
- Semantic tests: scenario 42 extended (`OHP2` now covers `C2160` to `C2163`).

### Chunk 3, labels for the pilot divisions

- 59 existing rows labelled; no new claims, places or references. Old value of each activity column: blank. New values and reasons are in each row's `Activity:` note. Rows of the 4th Canadian Armoured (18), 1st Polish Armoured (31), British 1st Airborne (2), British 50th (6) and French 3rd Algerian (2) Divisions; 53 stated and 6 inferred.
- Rubric as before: the division fighting, capturing, landing or relieving another division in the line is `in_line` (point, stated); orders, arrivals or part of the division in action are `in_line` inferred (0.3 cap); command changes stay blank (`C1339`, `C1560`).
- Spans: `C1340` (Suitcase, 20 October 1944) is an `in_line` start and ends the 4th Canadian Armoured refit span of 4 September (`C1818`); `C1342` (Army Reserve at Boxtel, 23 December 1944) is a `reserve` start, ended by the `in_line` start `C1389` (Elephant, 26 January 1945); `C1579` (1st Polish Armoured at Wilhelmshaven, 6 May 1945) is a `garrison` start. The British 1st Airborne refit (`C1816`) and the 1st Polish Armoured line span (`C1553`) stay open (no dated end in the sources).
- Semantic tests: scenario 26, 4th Canadian Armoured on 1 December 1944: old expectation no answer (open refit span past 30 days); new expectation `in_line`, certain, inferred, 0.3 (the Suitcase line span ended by the Boxtel reserve); reason: the pilot rows are now labelled. Scenario 42 counts 63 rows and checks the new spans.

## 31 October 2026: FMS open-question decisions (branch fms-decisions-oct31)

- `C0957` event definition changed from "the takeover of 15 November" to "the arrival of 15 November". Reason: FMS B-811 p. 2 dates the takeover to 22 November (`C2127`); CMH supports the arrival only. Date, place and confidence unchanged.
- Decisions recorded in `docs/roadmap.md` (Scott, 31 October 2026): no command relations from a single manuscript; no Allied dates from the manuscripts; no reading of German duplicate copies or map sheets; strengths kept in notes only; no tracing of map front lines. No data changed by these.

## fms-b078-armygroupg: Army Group G in Lorraine and the Vosges from Foreign Military Studies B-078 (von Mellenthin; 31 October 2026)

Scott approved the reading of B-078 and a data branch on 31 October 2026. One branch from `main` `1c2f1bb`. Source: Friedrich Wilhelm von Mellenthin (chief of staff of Army Group G to 3-4 December 1944), B-078, *Kaempfe in Lothringen und in den Vogesen im Herbst 44, Teil IV* (signed Oberursel, 6 June 1946); German typescript, no English translation; NARA M1035 fiche 0078G, read on Fold3, where the set is mis-titled "A-999 to A1000, B018 and B078". A US Government work; the German text is paraphrased in English in the claims. The reading notes stay private and are not in this repository.

- **New reference** `R770` (B-078; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). Reliability: 22 of 27 checkable facts agree with the dataset, Ellis, de Lattre and the Nineteenth Army FMS notes (81%); after the CMH check (Riviera to the Rhine, The Lorraine Campaign) 25 of 27 (93%). Note text changed from 81% to 93% for that reason.
- **New places** `PL1045` to `PL1054` (10; OpenStreetMap Nominatim): Bad Bergzabern, Sankt Ingbert, Orscholz, Büdingen (Merzig), Hellimer, Bitche, Wissembourg, Rixheim, Schirmeck and Merzig.
- **New claims** `C2137` to `C2159` (23; each note carries old value none, new value and reason): headquarters flags from the situation maps (Army Group G at Bergzabern on 3 December; the First Army at St. Ingbert on 20 November and 3 December, with Obstfelder in command by early December; the Nineteenth Army at Gebweiler on 20 November); division and corps positions on 20 November (416th in the Orscholz switch, 19th and the 25th Panzergrenadier at Büdingen, 36th south of St. Avold, 11th Panzer on the Hellimer line); the 17th SS relieving the 559th south-east of St. Avold (night 22/23 November, 0.3); the 553rd gathering at Bitsch after the Zabern breakout (0.3); the LXXXIX Corps at Weissenburg from 25 November; the 245th arriving there from 27 November (0.3); the Panzer Lehr counterattack of 24 November and its withdrawal on 30 November; Panzer Brigade 106 stopped at Rixheim on 25 November; the 708th pushed over the Bruche valley about 25 November (0.3); the 21st Panzer behind the Saar at Saarlautern on 2 December; the 719th, LXXXII Corps (Hahm), XIII SS Corps (Simon) and 361st positions of 3 December.
  - The parent is blank on seven rows (`C2141` to `C2143`, `C2148`, `C2150`, `C2153`, `C2154`; inferred value: old none, new blank, reason: no relation covers the day, or B-078 differs from the relations table).
- **Changed rows** (old value, new value and reason in each row's Review sentence):
  - `C1012` (Panzer Lehr at Sarre-Union, 23 November) and `C1033` (256th VG at Haguenau, 26 November): `R770` added; event support one to two families; confidence 0.5 to 0.7.
  - Relations `CR0708`, `CR0710` and `CR0715`: valid_to_earliest moved to the new last listed claim (1944-11-01 to 1944-11-25; 1944-11-07 to 1944-12-03; 1944-11-23 to 1944-11-30); valid_to unchanged.
- **Command relation evidence**: 16 observed_during links for the new rows with a parent. No new relation in the first commit (see the CMH check below for `CR1064` to `CR1066`). No conflict group (next stays `G034`).
- **Semantic scenario 41**; earlier batch counts exclude `C2137` to `C2159`.
- Open points in `docs/roadmap.md` (FMS B-078 batch).
- **CMH check of the three disagreements** (second commit, 31 October 2026; Scott asked for it):
  - 553rd VG Division: confirmed by CMH Riviera to the Rhine ch. 21 p. 373 (`R652`). `CR0725` (LXIV Corps) valid_to 1945-01-04 to 1944-11-19 (basis inferred_from_next_claim to narrative; link from `C1028` changed from observed_during to asserts_end); new relation `CR1064` (LXXXIX Corps, 20 November 1944 to 4 January 1945); `C2148` parent blank to `GER-COR-089C` (confidence stays 0.3, inferred arrival day).
  - 245th Division: confirmed by CMH Riviera ch. 21 p. 379 (`R652`), Lorraine ch. 12 p. 547 (`R653`) and Riviera ch. 26 p. 467. `CR0739` (LXVII Corps, Holland) valid_to 1945-01-06 to 1944-11-26; new relation `CR1065` (LXXXIX Corps, 27 November 1944 to 6 January 1945); `C2150` parent blank to `GER-COR-089C` (stays 0.3, arrival).
  - 719th Division: confirmed by CMH Lorraine ch. 12 p. 535 (`R653`). `CR0641` (LXXXVIII Corps, Holland) valid_to 1944-12-14 to 1944-12-02; new relation `CR1066` (LXXXII Corps, 3 to 14 December 1944); `C2154` parent blank to `GER-COR-082C`, confidence 0.5 to 0.7; Context note on the Lexikon month row `C1061` (no value changed).
  - Higher Command Vosges: CMH Riviera ch. 21 p. 378 places it only "in the Strasbourg area" and names Feldkommandantur 987 at Haguenau among its elements; it does not name the command post. `C1034` is left unchanged.
  - No new reference (the CMH chapters are `R652` and `R653`); next relation id `CR1067`.

## fms-b701-armygroupb: Army Group B from Foreign Military Studies B-701 (Reichhelm; 30 October 2026)

Scott approved the reading of the Army Group B manuscript and a data branch on 30 October 2026. One branch from `main` `535b12c`. Ids start after the branches `fms-b703-armygroupg` (merged as PR #56; `C2116` to `C2126`, `PL1032` to `PL1038`, `R767`, `G033`) and `fms-b811-15army` (merged as PR #57; `C2127` to `C2134`, `PL1039` to `PL1044`, `R768`); the branch `fold3-links` (merged as PR #55) changes citations only. Source: Günther Reichhelm (Ia of Army Group B), B-701, *Summary of Army Group B Engagements from the middle of Oct 44 until the middle of Apr 45* (English translation, dated 29 November 1947); NARA M1035, read on Fold3. A US Government work, paraphrased in English in the claims. The reading notes stay private and are not in this repository. The maps (images 75-79) and the German section were not read.

- **New reference** `R769` (B-701; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). Reliability: 20 of 25 checkable facts agree with the dataset, Ellis and the B-811 and B-703 notes (80%). Good for the Ardennes order of battle; Allied dates are often a day off.
- **New places**: none (next place id stays `PL1045`).
- **New claims** `C2135` and `C2136` (each note carries old value none, new value and reason):
  - Fifteenth Army ends its withdrawal across the Scheldt by 23 September 1944, about 82,000 men and 580 guns (`C2135`, Walcheren `PL0549`, 0.7: B-701 p. 8 and Ellis vol. II p. 69 agree on the men; guns 580 against 530).
  - II SS Panzer Corps (2nd and 9th SS Panzer Divisions) in the Sixth Panzer Army order of battle on 16 December 1944 (`C2136`, representative point in the Eifel `PL0573`, 0.3; parent blank, inferred value: old none, new blank, reason: only B-701 states it, and `CR0755` has the corps under the army group to 2 January).
- **Changed rows**: none.
- **Command relation evidence**: `CR0598` gains an observed_during link for `C2135`. No new relation (next id stays `CR1064`).
- **Semantic scenario 40** (38 and 39 are used by the other two FMS branches); earlier batch counts exclude `C2135` and `C2136`.
- Open points in `docs/roadmap.md` (FMS B-701 batch).

## fms-b811-15army: Fifteenth Army from Foreign Military Studies B-811 (von Zangen; 30 October 2026)

Scott approved the reading of the Fifteenth Army and Army Group B manuscripts and a data branch on 30 October 2026. One branch from `main` `535b12c`. Ids start after the branch `fms-b703-armygroupg` (merged as PR #56; `C2116` to `C2126`, `PL1032` to `PL1038`, `R767`, `G033`); the branch `fold3-links` (merged as PR #55) changes citations only. Source: Gustav von Zangen, B-811, *Fifteenth Army (22 Nov 44-9 Mar 45): Defense Battles at the Roer and Rhine*, parts I-V (English translation; parts I-IV dated 15 November 1947, part V 20 December 1947); NARA M1035, read on Fold3. A US Government work, paraphrased in English in the claims. The reading notes stay private and are not in this repository. The German copy and the German maps were not read.

- **New reference** `R768` (B-811; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). Reliability: 18 of 21 checkable facts agree with the dataset and Ellis (86%). Good for corps and division sectors, reliefs and command-change dates; weak on Allied identifications.
- **New places** `PL1039` to `PL1044` (6; OpenStreetMap Nominatim): Königshoven (Bedburg), Bleibuir, Linnich, Effeld, Heinsberg and Schöneseiffen.
- **New claims** `C2127` to `C2134` (8; each note carries old value none, new value and reason):
  - Fifteenth Army takes over the Aachen-Roer front as Group von Manteuffel on 22 November 1944 at Königshoven (`C2127`, 0.5). CMH `C0957` keeps the arrival of 15 November; the two rows are different events.
  - LXXIV Corps to the Fifteenth Army about 10 December (`C2128`, Bleibuir, 0.5).
  - XLVII Panzer Corps hands over its Linnich sector and leaves for the Ardennes on 4 December (`C2129`, 0.5).
  - 59th Infantry Division arrives north of Heinsberg about 4 December (`C2130`, 0.3).
  - Corps group Felber takes over the east of the XII SS Corps sector by 17 December; XII SS Corps at Effeld (`C2131`, 0.3).
  - 176th Division back behind the Roer by about 28 January 1945 (`C2132`, 0.3).
  - 62nd Volksgrenadier Division relieves the 246th near Monschau about 25 January (`C2133`, 0.3; inferred value: old none, new 25 January, reason: the night of 24/25 January).
  - 12th Volksgrenadier Division reaches Schöneseiffen about 2 February (`C2134`, 0.3).
  - The parent is blank on the seven rows other than `C2127` (inferred value: old none, new blank, reason: the change is stated only by B-811 and is not yet in the command relations table).
- **Changed rows** (old value, new value and reason in each row's Review sentence):
  - `C1089` (LXVII Corps to the Sixth Panzer Army, 17 January): `R768` added; event support one to two families; date and confidence unchanged.
  - `C1140` (LVIII Panzer Corps under the Fifteenth Army, February): `R768` added as a second family; confidence stays 0.3 (approximate day).
- **Command relation evidence**: `CR0598` gains an observed_during link for `C2127`. No new relation (next id stays `CR1064`).
- **Semantic scenario 39** (38 is used by `fms-b703-armygroupg`); earlier batch counts exclude `C2127` to `C2134`.
- Open points in `docs/roadmap.md` (FMS B-811 batch).

## fms-b703-armygroupg: Army Group G from Foreign Military Studies B-703 (Wilutzky; 30 October 2026)

Scott approved the reading of the Army Group G manuscripts and a data branch on 30 October 2026. One branch from `main` `535b12c` (the branch `fold3-links`, merged as PR #55, changes only citations of existing rows and adds no ids). Source: Horst Wilutzky (Ia of Army Group G), B-703, *The Fighting of Heeresgruppe G in the West: the final battle in central and southern Germany until the surrender (22 Mar-6 May 1945)*, English translation, with Schulz's statement and Hausser's commentary; NARA M1035, read on Fold3. A US Government work, paraphrased in English in the claims. The reading notes stay private and are not in this repository. A-999 (Army Group G, 20 September-8 November 1944) was not found as separate pages in its Fold3 set and is left open.

- **New reference** `R767` (B-703; archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`). Reliability: 18 of 23 checkable facts agree with the dataset, Ellis and de Lattre (78%). Good for army group headquarters moves and subordination changes; weak and sometimes late on Allied crossings. Year written: the signature is dated 12 January 1947 but the preface says September-October 1947; 1947 is used.
- **New places** `PL1032` to `PL1038` (7; OpenStreetMap Nominatim): Michelstadt, Neuenstadt am Kocher, Waldenbuch, Wallerstein, Schierling, Ampfing and Hohenaschau. Inferred value: the manuscript's "Neuenstadt" is taken to be Neuenstadt am Kocher (the town of that name in the First Army zone).
- **New claims** `C2116` to `C2126` (11; each note carries old value none, new value and reason):
  - Army Group G headquarters: Michelstadt (24 March, 0.3, inferred arrival day; old none, new 24 March, reason: first full day after permission on 23 March), Neuenstadt (28 March), Waldenbuch (30 March), Schierling (22 April), Ampfing (26 April, 0.3; the night of 25/26 April), re-formed for the capitulation on 3 May (0.3, place fallback Hohenaschau).
  - Subordination changes stated only by B-703, parent left blank (inferred value: old none, new blank, reason: not yet in the command relations table): 16th Volksgrenadier Division to the Nineteenth Army (6 April), LXXX Corps to the Nineteenth Army (8 April), 198th Division to the First Army (18 April). All 0.3 (representative points).
  - Nineteenth Army back under the Commander-in-Chief West on 10-11 April (`C2124`, 0.3; linked to `CR0815`).
  - Seventh Army directly under the Commander-in-Chief West about 25 April (`C2126`, alternate, 0.3).
- **Changed rows** (old value, new value and reason in each row's Review sentence):
  - `C1198` (Schulz takes over, 5 April): place `PL0641` (representative point at Munich) to `PL1035` (Wallerstein); precision 50 to 5 km; location basis representative_point to documented_on_date; confidence 0.3 to 0.5; `R767` added. Reason: B-703 p. 29 names the command post of 5 April.
  - `C1199` (dissolution order, 29 April): place `PL0641` to `PL1038` (Hohenaschau); precision 50 to 10 km; basis representative_point to documented_on_date; confidence 0.3 to 0.5; parent blank to `GER-OBW-000H`; `R767` added. Reason: B-703 pp. 48-50 names the place and agrees with the Lexikon on the date.
  - `C1205` (Seventh Army under Kesselring, 28 April): conflict group blank to `G033`. Reason: B-703 dates the same transfer about 25 April; CMH stays preferred.
  - `C1231` (LXXXII Corps to the First Army, 9 April): `R767` added; event support one to two families; confidence stays 0.3 (representative point).
- **Command relation evidence**: `CR0594` gains observed_during links for `C2116` to `C2121` and `C1199`; `CR0815` for `C2124`. No new relation (next id stays `CR1064`).
- **New conflict group** `G033` (Seventh Army transfer, 25 vs 28 April).
- **Semantic scenario 38**; scenario counts of earlier batches exclude `C2116` to `C2126`.
- Open points in `docs/roadmap.md` (FMS B-703 batch).

## fms-19army: German Nineteenth Army and LXIV Corps from Foreign Military Studies B-766, B-263, B-463, B-468 and B-550 (30 October 2026)

Scott approved the 36 proposed changes on 30 October 2026 after a read-only check of the five manuscripts against the dataset and de Lattre (`R686`). One branch from `main` `664d9eb`. Sources: Botsch, B-766 (16 September-17 November 1944) and B-263 (8 November 1944-10 January 1945); Brandstaedter, B-463 (4 January-21 March 1945); Thumm, B-468 (1 November-31 December 1944) and B-550 (6 December 1944-28 January 1945); NARA M1035, read on Fold3. These are US Government works, and the claims paraphrase them in English. The reading notes stay private and are not in this repository.

- **New references** `R762` (B-766), `R763` (B-263), `R764` (B-463, strength alias `FMS B-463`), `R765` (B-468) and `R766` (B-550). All are archive_document, family `fms_german_manuscript`. Reliability (share of checkable facts that agree):
  - B-766: 70%.
  - B-263: 62%.
  - B-463: 62%.
  - B-468: 60%.
  - B-550: 40%.

  All five are good for German dates, headquarters and orders of battle, and poor for Allied unit identifications. B-468 and B-550 are used for German facts only. Arrival and departure dates are capped at 0.3.
- **New places** `PL1016` to `PL1031` (16; OpenStreetMap Nominatim): Guebwiller, Kirchhofen, Widensolen, Biffontaine, Illfurth, Orbey (Urbeis), Sigolsheim, Engen, Le Ménil, Remiremont, Éloyes, Hérimoncourt, Burnhaupt-le-Haut, Riedwihr, Ribeauvillé and Munster. The approval estimated about 9; seven more were needed because places such as Remiremont and Munster were not yet in the table. Inferred value: "Urbeis" in B-263 and B-550 is taken to be Orbey (German Urbeis), not Urbeis in the Bas-Rhin.
- **New claims** `C2078` to `C2115` (38). Each note carries old value (none), new value and reason.
  - 29 from the check:
    - Nineteenth Army headquarters at Guebwiller (6 October 1944 move; 4 January 1945) and Kirchhofen (5 February 1945).
    - LXIV Corps at Remiremont (22 September), Biffontaine (22 October), Colmar (6 December), Ribeauvillé (13 December attack) and Widensolen (command post on 26 January 1945; Thumm relieved and Grimmeiss named on 28 January).
    - LXVI Corps to the Fifth Panzer Army (29 September); LXXXIX Corps under the army (16 October); LXIII Corps on the Ill south-west of Illfurth (about 26 November, 0.3); XC Corps taken from the army (6 December).
    - 198th Division: Éloyes (25 September, 0.3), Dannemarie (21 November), Sélestat (27 November arrival, 0.3) and departure (17 March 1945, 0.3).
    - 189th merged into the 198th (27 September, 0.3); 189th at Sigolsheim (December).
    - 269th arrives (31 October, 0.3). 716th relieves the 16th at Saint-Dié (14 November). 16th VG at Orbey (24 December).
    - Panzer-Brigade 106 arrives at Belfort (1 October, 0.3).
    - French 4th Moroccan Mountain identified at Le Ménil (6 October, 0.3). French 1st Armoured at Plancher-les-Mines (28 September, 0.7, de Lattre and B-766).
