## fms-b466-geyr: German positions and command dates from Foreign Military Studies MS B-466 (30 October 2026)

Scott approved the data changes on 30 October 2026 after a read-only check of the source against the dataset and Ellis (vol. I). One branch from `main` `c8a077f`. Source: Geyr von Schweppenburg, *Die Invasion (Geschichte der Panzergruppe West)*, FMS MS B-466 (1947), NARA M1035, read on Fold3 (German typescript, printed pages; annexes by number). A US Government work; the claims paraphrase it in English. The reading notes stay private and are not in this repository.

- **New reference** `R761` (FMS B-466; source type archive_document, new source family `fms_german_manuscript`). Reliability note: 30 of 42 checkable facts agree with the dataset or Ellis; arrival dates of distant reinforcements run 1-3 weeks early, so arrival rows from it are capped at 0.3.
- **New places** `PL1005` to `PL1015` (Évreux, Thouars, Montauban, Bordeaux, Nîmes, Toulouse, Bérigny, Hérouvillette, Torigni-sur-Vire, Nogent-le-Rotrou, Abbaye d'Ardenne), OpenStreetMap Nominatim.
- **New claims** `C2060` to `C2077` (18):
  - Stations before D-Day from Annex 1 (from map, approximate; dated 6 June 1944 under `OP-NEPTUNE`, inferred value: the annex is undated beyond "before D-Day"): 12th SS Panzer (Évreux, 0.5), 17th SS Panzergrenadier (Thouars, 0.5), 1st SS Panzer (Antwerp, 0.3), 2nd SS Panzer (Montauban, 0.5), 11th Panzer (Bordeaux, 0.5), 9th Panzer (Nîmes, 0.5), LVIII (Reserve) Panzer Corps (Toulouse, headquarters representative point, 0.3), Panzer Lehr (Nogent-le-Rotrou, 0.3; Ellis and `C0038` have Chartres), 2nd Panzer (Abbeville, 0.3; Ellis and `C0064` have Amiens). The last two are separate event rows, not conflict groups, because the other rows have later dates.
  - `C2069` Panzer Lehr assembly at Aunay-sur-Odon, 8 June 1944, 0.5, alternate in the new conflict group `G029` with `C0053` (Thury-Harcourt, preferred).
  - Caen sector and Normandy front: `C2070` 21st Panzer command post at Saint-André-sur-Orne, 10 June (0.5); `C2071` 12th SS (25th SS Panzergrenadier Regiment) command post at the Abbaye d'Ardenne, 10 June (0.5; the abbey's identity is inferred, Geyr writes "the abbey north of Caen"); `C2072` 3rd Fallschirmjäger advance detachment at Bérigny, 11 June (0.5); `C2073` 12th SS line pulled back at Le Mesnil-Patry, 16 June (0.5; unit inferred from the sector); `C2074` 21st Panzer attack stalls at Hérouvillette, 16 June (0.5).
  - Arrivals, capped at 0.3: `C2075` 2nd SS Panzer held as army reserve at Torigni-sur-Vire, 17 June; `C2076` LXXXVI Corps takes over the Seine-Orne sector, 20 June 08:00 (activity in_line, span start); `C2077` II SS Panzer Corps reaches the Alençon area by 24 June.
- **Changed rows** (each carries a `Review (FMS B-466 batch, 30 October 2026 ...)` sentence with old value, new value and reason):
  - `C0053` (Panzer Lehr, Thury-Harcourt, 8 June 1944): conflict_group_id blank to `G029`; status stays preferred, confidence unchanged. Reason: B-466 places the same assembly at Aunay-sur-Odon (new alternate `C2069`).
  - B-466 added as a further source (`reference_ids` gains `R761`, `source` gains the Fold3 link, `source_locator` gains the page) on 18 rows whose date and place it confirms; no confidence changed: `C0004`, `C0033`, `C0060`, `C0068`, `C0089`, `C0091`, `C0092`, `C0148`, `C0187`, `C0188`, `C0196`, `C0201`, `C0203`, `C0768`, `C0769`, `C0770`, `C0771`, `C0793`. Old and new `reference_ids` are in each row's note.
- **Checks.** Semantic scenario 36 (6 expectations); the new rows are excluded from the batch counts of scenarios 26 to 35 (`BATCH36`, like `BATCH35`). One coordinate exception: the 12th SS Panzer Division's overnight march of about 110 km from its station near Évreux (`C2060`) to the Caen assembly areas (`C0033`). Validator warnings unchanged (42). who_controls grid: 12,460 answers, 4 changed, 0 new keys, all activity lines only (LXXXVI Corps in_line carried forward from `C2076` on 3, 10 and 17 July 1944; Panzer Lehr reserve point from `C2067` on 6 June 1944); no command answer changed.
- **Not added (deferred):** the 8th Werfer Brigade under I SS Panzer Corps north-east of Vire, night 27/28 June 1944 (p. 48): no unit row exists. XLVII Panzer Corps and I SS Panzer Corps before D-Day (Annex 1): the map labels are not read with certainty. Recorded in `docs/roadmap.md`.
- **Disagreements recorded, no value changed:** Panzer Group West takes command on the evening of 8 June in B-466 against 9 June in `C0059` and Ellis; Eberbach takes over on the evening of 5 July in B-466 against 4 July in `C0717` and Ellis; Caumont falls on 12 June in B-466, the date of the alternate `C0086`, against 13 June in `C0099`; Marcks's death attributed to the US 101st Airborne in B-466 against an air attack in `C0081`; the 1st SS Panzer Division east of Évrecy about 20 June in B-466 against 28 June to 9 July in Ellis and `C0772`.

## delattre-ellis-decisions: Scott's answers to the six open points of the de Lattre and Ellis batch (30 October 2026)

Scott accepted all six recommendations on 30 October 2026 ("Let's take your recommendations for the questions"). One branch from `main` `ca1a105`. Printed pages of de Lattre, *The History of the French First Army* (1952), paraphrase only.

- **Retired claim.** French 2nd Moroccan Infantry Division, Briançon, 14 October 1944 (Wikipedia, confidence 0.3), the first entry in `data/retired_claims.csv`. Old value: a preferred row at Briançon on 14 October; new value: retired, replaced by the new row below. Reason: de Lattre (pp. 130-131, 161, 187, 191) puts the division's Alpine fighting in mid-September 1944 and the division on the Doubs front by late September, so the 14 October date does not fit.
- **New claim.** French 2nd Moroccan Infantry Division, Abriès (Queyras, Hautes-Alpes), 15 September 1944, de Lattre p. 161 (with pp. 130-131 for the Alpine context): the commander of the division's 3rd Moroccan Tabor Group is killed near Abriès. Representative point for the Alpine sector, precision 10 km, confidence 0.3, activity in_line (inferred, capped at 0.3).
- **Places.** New place Abriès, Hautes-Alpes (OpenStreetMap Nominatim). The place Briançon, Hautes-Alpes is removed: its only claim was retired (old row: Briançon, Hautes-Alpes, point 44.8984, 6.6436, precision 3 km, FR, Provence-Alpes-Côte d'Azur, OpenStreetMap Nominatim); its id is not to be reused.
- **Kept, no value changed.** The Rouffach row of 5 February 1945 (the one-day difference with de Lattre stays stated in the row); the German 15th Panzergrenadier (Saarbrücken) and 9th Panzer (Arnhem) Lexikon rows of 15 October 1944 at 0.3 beside the Venlo rows; the British 5th Infantry Division unit record.
- **No row, by decision.** Constance on 26 April 1945 for the French 1st Armoured Division (entered by a corps group of the I Corps, which is not modelled); the German 269th Division (no firm day in de Lattre). Recorded in `docs/roadmap.md`.

## source-fixes-delattre-ellis: corrections and new positions from the de Lattre and Ellis reading notes (30 October 2026)

Scott approved this batch on 30 October 2026 ("Please proceed"), after the reading-method branch was merged. One branch from `main` `647d774`. Sources: de Lattre, *The History of the French First Army* (1952), printed pages and paraphrase only, and Ellis, *Victory in the West*, vol. II, printed pages. The reading notes stay private and are not in this repository. Every changed row carries a `Review (source fixes from de Lattre and Ellis, 30 October 2026 ...)` sentence with the old value, the new value and the reason.

### Changed rows

- French 2nd Moroccan Infantry Division, Briançon, 14 October 1944: confidence 0.5 to 0.3, de Lattre added. Reason: de Lattre has the division retake Briançon and Modane by mid-September (pp. 130-131, 161) and its regiments on the Doubs and Lure front by late September (pp. 187, 191). Kept as an open question.
- French 4th Moroccan Mountain Division, 12 January 1945: place Vieux-Thann to Lure; location basis inferred to documented on date; confidence 0.3 to 0.5; activity transit (stated) to reserve (inferred). Reason: de Lattre p. 342 has the division still at Lure on 12 January (in army reserve since 20 December, p. 299); it attacks at Vieux-Thann only on 20 January (p. 345, an existing row).
- French 2nd Moroccan Infantry Division, Karlsruhe, 3 April 1945: conflict group blank to new group 28, status preferred to alternate, confidence 0.5 to 0.3. Reason: de Lattre pp. 434-437 has the division at Bruchsal on 2 and 3 April and gives Karlsruhe on 4 April to the 9th Colonial Division. The new Bruchsal row is the preferred row of the group (a coordinate exception documents the deliberate conflict).
- French 5th Armoured Division, Dannemarie, 24 November 1944: activity reserve to in_line (inferred, point). Reason: de Lattre p. 268 has its combat command 4 clearing the Seppois roads and reaching Altkirch that day; the evening relief of p. 275 is a regrouping for the next attack. Semantic scenario 33 expectation changed to match.
- French 2nd Armoured Division, Grussenheim, 28 January 1945: de Lattre added (pp. 357, 363-364), confidence 0.5 to 0.7. Reason: de Lattre names Vésinet's combat command of the division with the legion battalions of the 1st Free French Division at Grussenheim on 28 January, a second source family for place and date; not 0.9 because one combat command was engaged.
- French 4th Moroccan Mountain Division, Rouffach, 5 February 1945: de Lattre added (pp. 389, 391); date and place kept. De Lattre puts the junction on the evening of 4 February; the one-day difference is stated in the row.
- French 4th Moroccan Mountain Division, Strasbourg, 16 April 1945: de Lattre added (pp. 453, 468); no other value changed. De Lattre supports the assembly at Strasbourg and the Kehl crossing.
- French 1st Armoured Division, Ulm, 23 April 1945: note only; de Lattre pp. 481, 485-486 confirm the arrival on the evening of 23 April; the capture on 24 April is a new row.
- German 15th Panzergrenadier Division, Saarbrücken, 15 October 1944 (month-level Lexikon row) and German 9th Panzer Division, Arnhem, 15 October 1944 (month-level Lexikon row): note only, no value changed; Ellis vol. II p. 159 moves both into Army Group B reserve near Venlo from 14 October (new rows). A coordinate exception documents the 15th Panzergrenadier speed warning.

### New rows (28 claims, 14 units; 17 places; 1 unit)

- French 1st Free French Division: L'Isle-sur-le-Doubs 15 September 1944 (p. 159), Ronchamp 4 October (p. 196), Plancher-les-Mines 20 November (p. 260), Giromagny 22 November (pp. 260, 269), Masevaux 26 November (p. 275).
- French 3rd Algerian Division: Longegoutte crest 8 October 1944 (p. 199), Saulxures on the Moselotte 9 October (p. 200), Cornimont about 14 October (p. 201, approximate date, 0.3), Gérardmer 20 November (p. 261), La Bresse 21 November (p. 261).
- French 9th Colonial Division: Leopoldshafen 3 April 1945 (p. 435), Karlsruhe 4 April (p. 436).
- French 1st Armoured Division: Tuttlingen 21 April 1945 (p. 471), Stockach 21 April (p. 472), Ulm 24 April (pp. 481, 485-486, 490).
- French 2nd Armoured Division: Sélestat line 4 December 1944 (p. 283).
- French 4th Moroccan Mountain Division: Cernay 4 February 1945 (p. 387).
- French 5th Armoured Division: Suarce 22 November 1944 (p. 266), Dannemarie 27 November (pp. 278-279).
- French 2nd Moroccan Division: Bruchsal 3 April 1945 (pp. 434, 437), preferred row of the new conflict group.
- German 338th Division: Longegoutte counter-attack 6 October 1944 (p. 199), Bretigney 14 November (pp. 229-230, death of General Oschmann).
- German LXIII Corps: Belfort-Dannemarie corridor 22 November 1944 (pp. 262-263; representative point, 0.3).
- German 198th Division: Seppois 26 November 1944 (p. 277), Grussenheim counter-attack 29 January 1945 (p. 364).
- German 15th Panzergrenadier and 9th Panzer Divisions: Venlo reserve area from 14 October 1944 (Ellis vol. II p. 159; representative point, approximate day, 0.3).
- British 5th Infantry Division (new unit record): Lübeck, 2 May 1945, behind the 11th Armoured Division (Ellis vol. II p. 337).
- Every new row has its activity value: 17 stated, 11 inferred; inferred values are capped at 0.3 and say why. No parent unit or command relation was added.
- New places, coordinates from OpenStreetMap Nominatim: Lure, Bruchsal, Tuttlingen, Stockach, Suarce, L'Isle-sur-le-Doubs, Ronchamp, Plancher-les-Mines, Giromagny, Masevaux, the Longegoutte forest (5 km), Saulxures-sur-Moselotte, Cornimont, La Bresse, Leopoldshafen, Bretigney, Seppois-le-Bas.

### Tests

- Semantic scenario 35 (7 expectations). Earlier scenarios that count batch rows now leave out this batch's new rows (old filter: rows not from the official-history pass; new filter: the same, less this batch; reason: the batch totals stay as approved). Scenario 34 now selects its rows by the official-history pass wording (old: every row outside the earlier filter). Scenario 33 keeps the two relabelled French rows in its count; its stated/inferred totals change from 45/19 to 44/20 because the Lure row is now inferred.

## official-history-pass-1: 8 May 1945 rows, span closures and blank divisions from the official histories (30 October 2026)

Scott approved the official-history pass on 30 October 2026, after the Canada and France batch was merged (PR #47). Branch `official-history-pass-1` from `main` `1431a82`. Each chunk is its own commit; every commit passes the full checks.

Sources: the US Army official history (CMH, The Last Offensive, chapters already cited), the Ninth Army history *Conquer: The Story of Ninth Army, 1944-1945* (1947, public domain; new reference `R760`), the Canadian official history by Stacey (`R668`, short quotations with printed pages) and de Lattre's history of the French First Army (`R686`, page numbers and paraphrase only; the reading notes stay outside the repository). Ellis's British official history is not available as public text and was not used.

Every row is a new claim with its activity values set; nothing existing was changed (old value of each activity column: none, the row is new). Rows dated 8 May 1945 whose place or state the source does not give for that day are inferred and capped at 0.3, and each says why in its `Activity:` note.

Semantic tests: scenarios 26 to 32 now count only their own batch's rows (`NOT_OHP`): old filter, every activity row of the batch's units; new filter, the same rows less those whose notes name the official-history pass; reason, this pass adds rows to units of those batches, and the batch totals stay as approved.

### Chunk 1, US divisions (Ninth Army history and The Last Offensive)

- New reference `R760`, Conquer: The Story of Ninth Army (Internet Archive, Public Domain Mark; family `us_army_unit_history`). Its short title is written "Conquer, The Story of Ninth Army, 1944-1945" because a leading "Conquer:" would read as a notes label.
- New places: `PL0981` Gorleben, `PL0982` Münster, `PL0983` Enns (Upper Austria), coordinates from OpenStreetMap Nominatim.
- 14 new claims, `C2003` to `C2016` (5 stated, 9 inferred):
  - C2003, 2nd Armored, 21 April 1945, Braunschweig: garrison start (stated), 0.5. Relieved on the Elbe, takes over a military government mission (Conquer p. 308).
  - C2004, 2nd Armored, 8 May 1945, Braunschweig: garrison point (inferred), 0.3. Assembled south of Braunschweig for Task Force Berlin (Conquer p. 334).
  - C2005, 30th Infantry, 21 April 1945, Magdeburg: in_line start (stated), 0.5. Relieves the 2nd Armored along the Elbe (Conquer p. 308).
  - C2006, 30th Infantry, 8 May 1945, Magdeburg: in_line point (inferred), 0.3. The Ninth Army holds the Elbe on order (Conquer pp. 330, 334).
  - C2007, 83rd Infantry, 8 May 1945, Barby: in_line point (inferred), 0.3. The Barby bridgehead is kept (Conquer pp. 308, 330; The Last Offensive p. 447).
  - C2008, 84th Infantry, 21 April 1945, Gorleben: in_line point (stated), 0.5. Reaches the Elbe at Gorleben (Conquer p. 309).
  - C2009, 84th Infantry, 8 May 1945, Gorleben: in_line point (inferred), 0.3. Holds part of the XIII Corps Elbe front (Conquer pp. 309, 330).
  - C2010, 29th Infantry, 8 May 1945, Münster: reserve point (inferred), 0.3. Assembled east of Münster while elements relieve the 95th Division in Bremen (Conquer pp. 334, 367).
  - C2011, 75th Infantry, 22 April 1945, Brambauer: garrison start (stated), 0.3. Its Ruhr occupation zone is enlarged (Conquer p. 309); this ends the rest span opened on 14 April (`C1909`) by the implicit-end rule.
  - C2012, 75th Infantry, 8 May 1945, Brambauer: garrison point (inferred), 0.3 (Conquer p. 334).
  - C2013, 104th Infantry, 8 May 1945, Halle: in_line point (inferred), 0.3. The Mulde line (The Last Offensive p. 448; Conquer p. 334).
  - C2014, 82nd Airborne, 8 May 1945, Ludwigslust: in_line point (inferred), 0.3. Holds the agreed line at Ludwigslust (The Last Offensive p. 463).
  - C2015, 65th Infantry, 8 May 1945, Enns: in_line point (inferred), 0.3. XX Corps on the Enns awaiting the Russians (The Last Offensive p. 466).
  - C2016, 103rd Infantry, 8 May 1945, Innsbruck: garrison point (inferred), 0.3. In Innsbruck after the Nineteenth Army surrender (The Last Offensive pp. 468, 471).
- Still without an 8 May 1945 row (no place in the sources read): the 70th, 101st and 106th; the 44th and 97th close their spans on 5 and 7 May (unchanged).

### Chunk 2, Canadian and French divisions (Stacey and de Lattre)

- New place: `PL0984` Sankt Anton am Arlberg (OpenStreetMap Nominatim).
- 6 new claims, `C2017` to `C2022`, all 8 May 1945 and inferred (0.3):
  - C2017 to C2020: the 2nd Canadian (Oldenburg), 3rd Canadian (Aurich), 4th Canadian Armoured (Varel) and 5th Canadian Armoured (Delfzijl) Divisions: garrison point. Stacey p. 610 dates the cease fire on the First Canadian Army front to 08:00 on 5 May; p. 597 (3rd Division before Aurich) and p. 594 (5th Armoured's last major operation at Delfzijl). The place is the division's last named place; holding occupied ground after the cease fire is read as garrison.
  - C2021, French 2nd Moroccan Infantry, Sankt Anton am Arlberg: in_line point. De Lattre pp. 500-501 (paraphrase): the column crosses the Arlberg from 5 to 7 May.
  - C2022, French 5th Armoured, Bregenz: garrison point. De Lattre pp. 498-499 (paraphrase): Bregenz and Dornbirn on 1 and 2 May; the German Twenty-fourth Army negotiates its surrender on 4 May.
- Still without an 8 May 1945 row: the French 1st Free French, 1st Armoured, 3rd Algerian, 9th Colonial, 10th, 27th Alpine and Gironde Divisions (de Lattre gives no place for them in the last days), and all nine British divisions listed in the roadmap (Ellis not available).

### Chunk 3, German divisions that had no activity value

- New places: `PL0985` Villingen, `PL0986` Dinslaken (OpenStreetMap Nominatim).
- 8 new claims, `C2023` to `C2030`, all points and stated (the source has the division holding or attacking a sector, which is line service):
  - C2023, 416th Infantry, 15 January 1945, Nennig: in_line, 0.5. Holds the Orscholz Switch; its replacement battalion counterattacks at Butzdorf and Tettingen (The Last Offensive p. 118).
  - C2024, 6th SS Mountain, 6 March 1945, Trier: in_line, 0.5. Counterattacks across the Ruwer south of Trier (The Last Offensive p. 242).
  - C2025, 180th Infantry, 24 March 1945, Wesel: in_line, 0.5. Holds the Rhine opposite the US 30th Division (The Last Offensive pp. 304, 308).
  - C2026, 2nd Fallschirmjäger, 24 March 1945, Dinslaken: in_line, 0.3 (place inferred). Holds the south of the LXIII Corps zone and is sent against the US 79th Division (The Last Offensive pp. 304, 315).
  - C2027, 6th Fallschirmjäger, 16 February 1945, Calcar: in_line, 0.5. Relieves the 84th Infantry Division between the Cleve-Calcar road and the Rhine (Stacey p. 484).
  - C2028, 712th Infantry, 23 December 1944, Kapelsche Veer: in_line, 0.5. Kapelsche Veer is brought into its sector (Stacey p. 452).
  - C2029, 719th Infantry, 24 April 1945, Villingen: in_line, 0.5. Counterattacks the French 27th Infantry Regiment south of Villingen (de Lattre p. 478, paraphrase).
  - C2030, 85th Infantry, 11 August 1944, Potigny: in_line, 0.3 (place inferred). Takes over the 12th SS Panzer Division's front south of Caen (Stacey p. 248). Operation: the Northern France campaign (the date is after the Normandy campaign).
- Still blank: the 226th Infantry Division (Stacey p. 332 places only some of its units in the Le Havre garrison; the main body is not placed) and the US 1st Airborne Task Force (not a division; no official-history row read).
- German refit spans: none added in this chunk (see the roadmap).
- Semantic scenario 34 (8 expectations).

## activity-rollout-can-fr: activity status for 3 Canadian and 10 French divisions (29 October 2026)

Scott approved the combined Canada and France batch on 29 October 2026, after German batch 3 was merged (PR #46). Branch `activity-rollout-can-fr` from `main` `7435b2f`.

Units (13): the 1st and 3rd Canadian Infantry and 5th Canadian Armoured Divisions; the French 1st Free French, 1st and 5th Armoured, 2nd Moroccan, 4th Moroccan Mountain, 9th Colonial, 10th, 14th and 27th Alpine Infantry Divisions and the Gironde Marching Division.

Sources: the references already cited by each row; for the new claims, the English Wikipedia article on the 4th Moroccan Mountain Division (R070) and de Lattre's history of the French First Army (R686). De Lattre is copyrighted: the new claims give page numbers and a paraphrase only, no quotation, and the reading notes stay outside the repository.

Rows: 64 activity rows (45 stated, 19 inferred): in_line 57, garrison 3, reserve 2, transit 2; spans: 62 point, 1 start, 1 end.

- 58 existing rows labelled (39 Canadian, 19 French). Each has the old value (blank), the new value and the reason in its notes. The 3rd Canadian Division's D-Day landing (6 June 1944) is a start, closed on 31 July 1944 when it was relieved after 55 days in the line. Brigade, battalion, combat-command and patrol rows are inferred under the main-body rule. Boulogne and Royan (fortresses besieged) are in_line; the 1st Canadian Division taking control of a German corps area on 8 May 1945 is garrison; the 10th Division policing the Colmar pocket and the 14th Division protecting the army's rear on 8 May 1945 are inferred garrison. Command changes, activations and one corps hand-over stay blank.
- 6 new claims, C1997-C2002:
  - C1997, 4th Moroccan Mountain Division, 12 January 1945, Vieux-Thann (PL0978): transit point (stated), 0.3. Moved from near Lure to the Thann-Cernay front (de Lattre p. 342; location inferred).
  - C1998, 4th Moroccan Mountain Division, 20 January 1945, Vieux-Thann (PL0978): in_line point (stated), 0.5. Attacks at Vieux-Thann, having relieved the 2nd Moroccan Infantry Division (de Lattre pp. 344-345).
  - C1999, 4th Moroccan Mountain Division, 16 April 1945, Strasbourg (PL0619): in_line point (stated), 0.3. Crosses the Rhine into Germany after some weeks of rest (Wikipedia; location inferred).
  - C2000, 4th Moroccan Mountain Division, 8 May 1945, Bregenz (PL0654): in_line point (inferred), 0.3. Ends the war in the Vorarlberg (Wikipedia).

## activity-rollout-german-1: activity status for 30 German divisions (29 October 2026)

Scott approved German batch 1 on 29 October 2026, after batch 3 was merged (PR #43). Branch `activity-rollout-german-1` from `main` `74caeb3`.

Units (30): the German divisions with the most claims that had no activity values: Panzer Lehr; 1st SS, 2nd SS and 12th SS Panzer; 17th SS Panzergrenadier; 2nd, 9th, 11th, 21st and 116th Panzer; 3rd, 15th and 25th Panzergrenadier; 3rd and 5th Fallschirmjäger; 91st Luftlande; 12th and 246th Volksgrenadier; 59th, 84th, 89th, 198th, 245th, 275th, 338th, 346th, 352nd and 353rd Infantry; 709th and 716th Static. The 9th and 10th SS Panzer Divisions already had values from the pilot and are left as they are.

Sources: only the references already cited by each row (US Army official histories, Ellis, Lexikon der Wehrmacht listings and Wikipedia). No new references, places or claims. No copyrighted text is added.

Rows: 148 existing rows labelled (122 stated, 26 inferred): in_line 117, transit 16, reserve 10, refitting 3, garrison 2. All are points: the German narrative rows are isolated dated events, and no source in hand gives both ends of a span.

- Each row has the old value (blank), the new value and the reason in its notes.
- Left blank on purpose (77 rows): monthly order-of-battle listings ("is listed in ... under ... Corps"), subordinate assignments, command changes, surrenders and destructions (not one of the five states), and alternate or disputed rows.
- Rules applied, as in the Allied batches: a fortress or besieged city defended (Cherbourg, Aachen) is in_line; a coastal sector far from the front is garrison; a Kampfgruppe, regiment or contingent in action gives an inferred in_line for the division (main-body rule); an assembly behind the front ready to be committed is inferred reserve; inferred values are capped at 0.3 in the answers.
- Next ids unchanged: claim C1997, place PL0978, reference R760, relation CR1064, conflict group G028.

## activity-rollout-3: activity status for the remaining 11 British divisions (29 October 2026)

Scott approved batch 3 on 29 October 2026, after batch 2 was merged (PR #42). Branch `activity-rollout-3` from `main` `6bbafb1`.

Units (11): the British 6th Airborne, 7th Armoured, 79th Armoured and Guards Armoured Divisions and the 3rd, 43rd (Wessex), 49th (West Riding), 51st (Highland), 52nd (Lowland), 53rd (Welsh) and 59th (Staffordshire) Infantry Divisions.

Sources: the English Wikipedia division articles (R082, R095, R066 and one new reference, R759, for the 43rd (Wessex) Division; CC BY-SA) and, for the 6th Airborne Division's relief on the Maas, the CMH combat chronicle of the US 75th Infantry Division (R746, public domain). No copyrighted text is in the repository.

Rows: 98 activity rows in all (89 stated, 9 inferred): in_line 91, refitting 3, garrison 3, transit 1; spans: 91 point, 4 start, 3 end.

- 89 existing rows labelled. Each one has the old value (blank), the new value and the reason in its notes. Follow-up landings and arrivals (Gold Beach, Juno Beach, "arriving in Normandy") and the two alternate rows stay blank. The 3rd Division's D-Day landing is a point, not a start, because no source closes it within 90 days. The 6th Airborne Division's D-Day landing is a start, closed on 26 August 1944 at Pont-Audemer (the row there says it was never out of the line since 6 June). The 7th Armoured Division's takeover of Hamburg on 3 May 1945 is a garrison start (inferred). Five rows for a brigade or battalion are inferred under the main-body rule (79th Armoured on D-Day, Guards Armoured at Cagny, 52nd at Walcheren and Waldfeucht, 59th on the Orne); one pursuit row is inferred (7th Armoured, 31 August 1944).
- 9 new claims, C1988-C1996, listed below.
- No new places. One new reference, R759 (Wikipedia: 43rd (Wessex) Infantry Division).
- Next ids: claim C1997, place PL0978, reference R760; relation CR1064 and conflict group G028 are unchanged.

Rules applied, as in batches 1 and 2: occupation is garrison; rest and training out of the line are refitting; a derived date lowers the confidence to 0.3; an inferred value is capped at 0.3; the main-body rule applies.

New claims:

- C1988, 6th Airborne Division, 1 September 1944, Pont-Audemer, Eure (PL0738): transit point (stated), 0.3. Returns to England after the Normandy campaign (the source says early September; the location is the point of departure, inferred).
- C1989, 6th Airborne Division, 26 December 1944, Dinant, Namur (PL0444): in_line start (stated), 0.5. Reaches its places in the defensive line between Dinant and Namur.
- C1990, 6th Airborne Division, 31 January 1945, Roermond, Limburg (PL0949): in_line start (stated), 0.3. Made responsible for the Maas between Venlo and Roermond (the source says the end of January).
- C1991, 6th Airborne Division, 21 February 1945, Roermond, Limburg (PL0949): in_line end (stated), 0.5. Relieved on the Maas by the US 75th Infantry Division (date from its combat chronicle); returns to England.
- C1992, 7th Armoured Division, 25 February 1945, Susteren, Limburg (NL) (PL0753): refitting point (stated), 0.3. Short rest for training (the source says late February; the location is inferred).
- C1993, 7th Armoured Division, 8 May 1945, Hamburg (PL0768): garrison end (inferred), 0.3. Holds Hamburg at the end of the war in Europe.
- C1994, 43rd (Wessex) Infantry Division, 29 August 1944, Vernon, Eure (PL0741): refitting point (stated), 0.3. Grounded after the Seine crossing; rests and receives reinforcements.
- C1995, 49th (West Riding) Infantry Division, 13 September 1944, Le Havre (PL0169): refitting point (stated), 0.3. A few days rest after Le Havre.
- C1996, 49th (West Riding) Infantry Division, 8 May 1945, Utrecht (PL0621): garrison point (inferred), 0.3. Liberation of Utrecht and Amsterdam after the German surrender.

Changed existing rows (old value blank in every case; the new value and reason are in each row's notes): 89 rows of the 11 divisions, as summarised above.

Scott accepted all batch-2 recommendations on 29 October 2026; the only data change is this one. Source: the CMH combat chronicle, 86th Infantry Division (R750). 1 new claim; no existing row changed; no new place or reference. Next ids: claim C1988, place PL0978, reference R759.

- C1987, 8 May 1945, Burghausen (PL0509): in_line point (stated), 0.3. The chronicle says the division "was securing the left flank of the XV Corps, when the war in Europe ended"; it names no place, so the command post at Burghausen that day (C0712) is used and the confidence stays at 0.3.

## activity-rollout-2: activity status for 30 more divisions (29 October 2026)

Scott approved batch 2 on 29 October 2026, after batch 1 was merged (PR #41). Branch `activity-rollout-2` from `main` `5929279`.

Units (30): the remaining 28 US divisions (17th Airborne; 9th, 11th, 13th, 16th and 20th Armored; 26th, 42nd, 44th, 63rd, 65th, 66th, 70th, 71st, 75th, 76th, 78th, 84th, 86th, 87th, 94th, 97th, 99th, 100th, 102nd, 103rd, 104th and 106th Infantry) and two British divisions (11th Armoured and 15th (Scottish) Infantry). The 1st Airborne Task Force and the combat commands are left out (they are not divisions).

Sources: for the US divisions, the US Army Center of Military History combat chronicles (public domain; Wayback copies; 25 new references R734-R758, and the existing R582 for the 13th Armored); for the British divisions, the English Wikipedia articles already in the data (R023, R029; CC BY-SA). No copyrighted text is in the repository.

Rows: 142 activity rows in all (124 stated, 18 inferred): in_line 114, refitting 14, garrison 10, reserve 4; spans: 63 start, 58 point, 21 end.

- 60 existing rows labelled (32 US, 28 British). Each one has the old value (blank), the new value and the reason in its notes. One entry into combat (the 100th Division, 12 November 1944, C0541) is a point, not a start, because no source closes it within 90 days.
- 82 new claims, C1905-C1986 (US 76, British 6), listed below.
- 24 new places, PL0954-PL0977 (OpenStreetMap Nominatim, R002); the Monschau row reuses PL0594.
- Next ids: claim C1987, place PL0978, reference R759; relation CR1064 and conflict group G028 are unchanged.

Rules applied, as in batch 1: occupation, military government and security missions are garrison; containing a fortress (Lorient, St-Nazaire, the Ruhr pocket) is in_line; rest, rehabilitation, reequipment and training out of the line are refitting (training alone is inferred); a derived date (for example the day after a dated action) lowers the confidence to 0.3; an inferred value is capped at 0.3; the main-body rule applies (a row for a part of the division gives an inferred value for the division).

Spans: 21 explicit ends close their starts; the other starts end implicitly at the next start. Still open (10; the 30-day horizon applies): 11th Armored (Meuse, 23 December 1944), 26th (Saarlautern, 29 January 1945), 65th (entry into combat, 9 March 1945), 70th (17 February 1945), 75th (Brambauer rest, 14 April 1945), 84th (Gouvy, 22 January 1945), 104th (Roer, 23 February 1945), 106th (St. Quentin, 15 March 1945), 11th Armoured (Wesel, 28 March 1945) and 15th (Scottish) (Caumont, 23 July 1944).

8 May 1945: 19 of the 30 divisions have a row. No row (the source does not place the division on that day): the 44th, 65th, 70th, 75th, 84th, 86th, 97th (the cease-fire order reached it on 7 May; that row closes its span), 103rd, 104th and 106th Infantry, and the 15th (Scottish).

Tests: scenario 28 (14 expectations; 438 in all).

## activity-rollout-1: 28th Infantry Division refit at Neufchâteau (29 October 2026)

Scott asked for this on 29 October 2026. Source: the CMH combat chronicle, 28th Infantry Division (R724). 2 new claims; no existing row changed; no new place or reference. Next ids: claim C1905, place PL0954, reference R734.

- C1903, 22 December 1944, Neufchâteau (PL0248): refitting start (stated), 0.5. The chronicle says the division withdrew to Neufchâteau for reorganization. Reorganization is recorded as refitting, because the design folds rest into refitting.
- C1904, 2 January 1945, Verdun (PL0361, a representative point for the Meuse line from Givet to Verdun, 50 km): refitting end (stated), 0.5. The move to the Meuse line ends the refit. It is recorded as the end of the refit, not an in_line start: the next state row is 95 days later, and the chronicle has rehabilitation after 6 March, so a start would overstate the line service.

The refit after the Hürtgen (before 16 December 1944) is still deferred to the official-history pass.

## activity-rollout-1 second-source pass: 8 May 1945 rows and span closures from the CMH combat chronicles (29 October 2026)

Scott accepted the follow-up's recommendations. This pass uses a second source family, the US Army Center of Military History combat chronicles (public domain; Wayback copies, R723, R724 and R726-R732), and for the 36th Division the Wikipedia article (R733). R725 was drafted and dropped before commit; it is unused and is not to be reused. 16 new claims (C1887-C1902), 3 new places (PL0951 Salzburg, PL0952 Kufstein, PL0953 Kitzbühel). Next ids: claim C1903, place PL0954, reference R734. No existing row changed.

8 May 1945 rows added (8 divisions):

- C1887 3rd Infantry Division: near Salzburg, in_line point (stated), 0.5.
- C1889 28th Infantry Division: occupation at Jülich and Kaiserslautern, garrison end (stated), 0.3; it closes C1888, garrison start, 7 April 1945.
- C1890 36th Infantry Division: Kitzbühel, in_line point (inferred: it relieved the 12th Armored on 4 May, and no source names its state on 8 May), 0.5; two source families.
- C1895 79th Infantry Division: occupation in the Dortmund area, garrison end (stated), 0.3.
- C1897 90th Infantry Division: en route to Prague, in_line point (stated), 0.3 (the place is inferred).
- C1898 7th Armored Division: junction with the Russians in Mecklenburg, in_line point (stated), 0.3 (Ludwigslust is a representative point).
- C1899 10th Armored Division: Innsbruck, in_line point (stated), 0.5.
- C1901 12th Armored Division: security duty after its relief by the 36th Division, garrison end (stated), 0.3; it closes C1900, garrison start, 4 May 1945 (Kufstein, inferred).

The 14th Armored already had an 8 May row (C1886). The follow-up report listed it as missing by mistake.

Still without an 8 May row: the 29th, 30th and 83rd Infantry Divisions and the 2nd Armored Division. Their chronicles stop before VE Day (the 29th on the Elbe until 4 May, the 30th at an Elbe contact, the 83rd leaving the Barby bridgehead on 6 May, the 2nd Armored halted on the Elbe on 20 April).

Spans closed (4):

- 79th: C1891 in_line end, 6 February 1945, closes C1832 (13 November 1944). The pass also adds C1892 refitting start, 7 February (the rest in February and March); C1893 in_line start, 24 March; and C1894 garrison start, 14 April.
- 83rd: C1896 in_line end, 2 March 1945, closes C1836.
- 14th Armored: C1902 in_line end, 2 May 1945 ("fired its last rounds"), closes C1850.
- 12th Armored: C1900 garrison start, 4 May 1945, ends C0630 implicitly.

Still open (6): the 5th and 8th Infantry entries into combat, the 30th's Rhine crossing, the 4th Armored reserve of 10 January 1945, the 7th Armored rest from 8 November 1944 and the 10th Armored attack of 20 February 1945. They are deferred to the official-history pass. Tests: scenario 27 has 4 more expectations.

## activity-rollout-1 follow-up: rest and refit periods, span closures and closing rows (29 October 2026)

Scott approved a follow-up pass on the same branch for the same 30 US divisions. 66 new claims (C1821-C1886), 13 new places (PL0938-PL0950; Blankenburg reuses PL0649), 5 new references (R718-R722, the Wikipedia articles of the 8th, 35th, 45th, 80th and 95th Infantry Divisions). Next ids: claim C1887, place PL0951, reference R723.

Source: the English Wikipedia article of each division, whose Second World War text follows the CMH combat chronicle. One source family per row, so confidence is 0.5 where the source gives a date and names the place, and 0.3 where the date or the place is inferred (each note says which). No row is 0.7 or 0.9. Every new row has an `Event support` quotation and an `Activity:` sentence.

Rules used:

- Occupation duty is recorded as `garrison`.
- A closing row at 8 May 1945 is added only where the source places the division on or until VE Day. It ends an open span (`end`) or is a `point`. 17 of the 30 divisions have one. The 3rd, 28th, 29th, 30th, 36th, 79th, 83rd and 90th Infantry Divisions and the 2nd, 7th, 10th and 12th Armored Divisions have none, because the source does not say where they were or what they were doing on that day.
- A batch-1 `start` whose implicit span would run for more than 90 days with no source is changed to a `point`.

Changes to existing rows (the reason is in each row's `Activity:` note):

- 2nd Infantry Division C0046, 5th Armored C0262, 6th Armored C0271, 35th Infantry Division C0424, 80th Infantry Division C0458: activity_span changed from start to point. The next state row is 104 to 262 days later.
- 95th Infantry Division C0661: point to start. The source has the division holding the Ruhr line until the end of the war. C1881 closes the span.
- 12th Armored Division C0630: point to start. This ends the St. Avold refit (C1848).

New claims:

Rest and refit periods and the line returns that end them (C1821-C1850):

- C1821 1st Infantry Division, 1944-12-07: refitting start (stated), confidence 0.3; moved to a rear area for refitting and rest after six months of combat.

## activity-rollout-1: activity status, rollout batch 1 (29 October 2026)

Scott approved the rollout on 29 October 2026 in batches of about 30 units. Batch 1 covers 30 US divisions: the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 5th, 8th, 9th, 28th, 29th, 30th, 35th, 36th, 45th, 69th, 79th, 80th, 83rd, 89th, 90th and 95th Infantry Divisions and the 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 5th, 6th, 7th, 8th, 10th, 12th and 14th Armored Divisions (the US divisions with the most rows; the pilot units are not touched).

113 existing rows are annotated (89 stated, 24 inferred): 110 in_line, 3 reserve, no refitting, garrison or transit. No new claim, place, relation or reference; next ids unchanged (C1821, PL0938). No location, date or confidence value changed. Every changed row has an `Activity:` note with the old value (blank), the new value and the reason. Row confidence is unchanged; activity confidence is computed at query time (`scripts/activity.py`).

Reading rules used in this batch:

- A row whose cited source describes the division itself fighting on the date (attacking, landing, capturing, defending) is `in_line`, `point`, `stated`.
- A row dating the division's entry into combat or into the line is `in_line`, `start`, `stated` (open; read as possible for at most 30 days).
- A row describing only part of the division (a combat command, task force, regiment, battalion or patrol) is `in_line`, `point`, `inferred` (main-body rule; capped at 0.3).
- A row naming reserve in words is `reserve` (`point`, or `start` for the 4th Armored pulled back into reserve on 10 January 1945).
- Command post moves, command changes and arrivals without combat are left blank: they do not show the state.
- The 6th Armored containing Lorient (15 August 1944) is `in_line`, not `garrison`; garrison is kept for the fortress garrisons.
- The 4th Armored's entry into combat (17 July 1944) is a point, not a start: a start would run unbroken to its reserve start of 10 January 1945.
- Disputed rows (89th Division Moselle crossing) are left blank.

## activity-status-pilot: activity status design and pilot (29 October 2026)

Design note: `docs/activity_status_design.md`. Three optional claim columns are added: `activity_status` (in_line, reserve, refitting, garrison, transit), `activity_basis` (stated, inferred) and `activity_span` (start, end, point). There is also a new note label, `Activity:`, a validator check (`check_activity`), `scripts/activity.py`, an `activity:` line in `who_controls`, and semantic scenario 26 (18 expectations). All 318 claim files gained the three blank columns, and existing rows are not backfilled.

Changes to existing rows (old value blank, new value as listed; the reason is in each row's `Activity:` note):

- 101st Airborne: C0571 in_line start (inferred) and C0573 refitting start (inferred).

- 2nd Canadian Division: C1820 refitting end at Dieppe, 6 Sep 1944 (Stacey).

## cleanup-3-brigades: Clean-up 3 after the brigade batches (29 October 2026)

## brigades-german-1944-45: Brigades Batch 3, German brigades, Kampfgruppen and fortress garrisons (29 October 2026)

Batch 3 of the 35 approved brigades (rows 20 to 35 of the planning list): 53 claims (`C1759` to `C1811`) for 19 German units. Sources: the US Army official histories (Cole, The Lorraine Campaign; Cole, The Ardennes; MacDonald, The Siegfried Line Campaign; MacDonald, The Last Offensive; Blumenson, Breakout and Pursuit; Clarke and Smith, Riviera to the Rhine) and Wikipedia. Relations `CR1040` to `CR1063`; places `PL0907` to `PL0936`; references `R701` to `R717`.

- **Panzer-Brigades 105, 106, 107, 108, 111, 112 and 113 (24 claims, C1759 to C1782).** The seven placeholders are now active, with formation dates (Wikipedia, List of German brigades).
- **Panzer-Brigade 150, the Führer-Begleit and Führer-Grenadier Brigades, SS-Panzer-Brigade Westfalen (11 claims, C1783 to C1793).** `GER-PZB-150B` is active. `GER-PG-FUEBE` and `GER-PG-FUEGR` are active, with their echelon changed from division to brigade and their names showing the division from 26 January 1945. `GER-SSPZB-WESTF` is new.
- **Kampfgruppen Chill, Peiper and von der Heydte (10 claims, C1794 to C1803).** These are new units. `GER-KG-PEIPER` is organic to the 1st SS Panzer Division (`CR1062`), and `GER-KG-CHILL` is organic to the 85th Infantry Division (`CR1061`). `GER-KG-HEYDTE` (Luftwaffe) has no relation.
- **Fortress garrisons (8 claims, C1804 to C1811).** The garrisons are their own category, with echelon `other`: `GER-FEST-LORIENT` (under the XXV Corps, `CR1063`, until 10 May 1945), `GER-FEST-STNAZ`, `GER-FEST-DUNKIRK` and `GER-FEST-LAROCH` (no relation).
- **Inferred values** are annotated in the rows: representative points (Albert Canal, Meuse-Escaut Canal), approximate dates (Woensdrecht 10 October, Dunkirk 9 September, La Rochelle 1 September, Führer-Grenadier reserve 31 December), relation starts at row dates, and relation ends taken from dissolution dates.
- **Panzer-Brigade 103 has no claims.** It served on the Eastern Front only.
- **Validator:** one new warning, `GER-PZB-108B` line 3 (an attachment to the 246th Volks Grenadier Division with no standing parent stated). It is explained in the row's Relationship note.
- **Semantic tests:** scenario 25 is new. Organic relations are kept out of `build/command_relations.csv` by design, so the Peiper and Chill checks read the master table.

## brigades-us-1944-45: Brigades Batch 2, US independent brigades, regiments, battalions and task forces (29 October 2026)

Batch 2 of the 35 approved brigades (rows 13 to 19 of the planning list). Each period and claim was checked against the US Army official histories (Clarke and Smith, Riviera to the Rhine; Blumenson, Breakout and Pursuit; Cole, The Ardennes; MacDonald, The Last Offensive) and Wikipedia; where the planning list (written from memory) differed from the sources, the sources were followed and the difference is listed below. Confidence follows the rubric (0.9 exact place and two source families; 0.7 exact place and one official history; 0.5 one family with an approximate place, or Wikipedia alone; 0.3 representative point or approximate date). Command relations: a corps or army headquarters is `assigned` (or `operational_control` where the source says "direct control"), a division or task force is `attached`; starts and ends not dated by a source are inferred from the row dates and annotated.

- **Dragoon airborne units (25 claims, C1714 to C1738).** New units `US-ABTF-001D` (1st Airborne Task Force, echelon division: a provisional airborne division), `US-PRCT-517` (517th Parachute Regimental Combat Team), `US-PIB-509` (509th Parachute Infantry Battalion) and `US-PIB-551` (551st Parachute Infantry Battalion). 1st Airborne Task Force (4): the Le Muy landing, the east-flank sector of 25 August, the southern third of the Alpine front in mid-October, the dissolution in November. 517th (11): Le Muy, Les Arcs, Saint-Cézaire, Col de Braus, Sospel, Soissons, Soy (1st Battalion), Manhay (3rd Battalion), Trois-Ponts under the 82nd Airborne Division, Stavelot under the 106th Division, Kleinhau under the 78th Division. 509th (4): Le Muy drop, Le Muy taken, the 3rd Armored Division sector, Sadzot. 551st (6): Le Muy drop, Draguignan, Nice, Werbomont, Rochelinval, inactivation at Juslenville. 16 new places; eight new references (R692 to R699). Inferred values, each annotated in the record (old value: none, the row is new; new value as stated; reason):
  - 1st Airborne Task Force, 25 August and mid-October 1944: representative points (Nice, the south end of the sector; Sospel), confidence 0.3; 15 October stands for "mid-October".
  - 1st Airborne Task Force, dissolution: 23 November 1944 (Wikipedia) is used; Clarke and Smith say about the 25th; Soissons (Wikipedia) is the place, confidence 0.3.
  - 509th, 23 December 1944: a representative point (Manhay) in the 3rd Armored Division sector, confidence 0.5.
  - The attachments to the 1st Airborne Task Force of the 517th and 509th close on 17 November 1944 and of the 551st on 21 November (the day before the attachment to the 101st Airborne Division that Wikipedia gives); division attachments in the Ardennes close the day before the next row under another division.
- **Corrections found against the sources (airborne):** the task force was named on 12 July 1944 (Clarke and Smith; Wikipedia 11 July) and dissolved in November 1944, so it is not in the Ardennes; the 551st was a separate battalion (1st Battalion, 551st Parachute Infantry), inactivated on 27 January 1945, not carried into the spring; Draguignan fell on 16 August (Clarke and Smith; Wikipedia says 15 August); the 517th was with the 13th Airborne Division from 15 February 1945, which ends its independent period (no row after 5 February); a planned row for the 551st raid at "Noirfontaine" (27-28 December 1944, Wikipedia) was dropped because the only place of that name found (Noirefontaine near Bouillon) is far from the 82nd Airborne front.

- **1st Special Service Force, Task Force Butler and Task Force A (20 claims, C1739 to C1758).** New units `US-SSF-001B` (1st Special Service Force, recorded as US; the Canadian component is not split), `US-TF-BUTLER` (Task Force Butler, officially the Provisional Armored Group, VI Corps) and `US-TDB-001B` (Task Force A, whose headquarters was the 1st Tank Destroyer Brigade). 1st Special Service Force (5): Levant, Port-Cros, Villeneuve-Loubet, the Alpine front in mid-October, the disbandment near Villeneuve-Loubet. Task Force Butler (6): Le Muy, Sisteron, Aspres, Marsanne, the Condillac Pass, Loriol. Task Force A (9): Miniac, Châteauneuf, Saint-Brieuc, Guingamp, Morlaix, Saint-Michel-en-Grève, Paimpol, the base of the Crozon peninsula, Douarnenez. 17 new places; one new reference (R700). `C1758` (Douarnenez, 20 September 1944) is in `data/operation_exceptions.csv` under the Siege of Brest, one day after its end, because Blumenson calls it the final action of the Brest operations. Inferred values, each annotated in the record (old value: none, the row is new; new value as stated; reason):
  - 1st Special Service Force, mid-October 1944: a representative point (Menton), confidence 0.3; 15 October stands for "mid-October".
  - 1st Special Service Force, disbandment: 5 December 1944 near Villeneuve-Loubet (Wikipedia) is used; Clarke and Smith give 6 January 1945 as the formal date. The attachment to the 1st Airborne Task Force ends on 23 November 1944 with the task force.
  - The Seventh Army's direct control of the force (and of the 1st Airborne Task Force) is recorded as `operational_control` (Clarke and Smith, ch. 3, p. 45: "direct control of the Seventh Army"); for the force it closes on 25 August, the day before the first row with the task force.
  - Task Force Butler: VI Corps from its assembly on 17 August (row date); under the 36th Division from 23 August (Clarke and Smith: Dahlquist dissolved it as a separate entity that morning but used it again); both relations close at the last row (29 August).
  - Task Force A, 27 August 1944: a representative point (Crozon) for the base of the peninsula, confidence 0.3; the attachment to the 8th Division starts on 15 September 1944 (inferred: Blumenson says mid-September). Task Force B (21-31 August), which combined it with the 38th Infantry, is not a unit in the dataset.
- **Corrections found against the sources (force and task forces):** the 1st Special Service Force landed on Levant and Port-Cros after midnight, so on 15 August (Wikipedia says 14 August), and was not under VI Corps but under the Seventh Army, then the 1st Airborne Task Force; Task Force Butler's life ends with the Montélimar battle (29 August), not with a later pursuit, and from 23 August it was under the 36th Division; Task Force A was a Third Army task force under VIII Corps built on the 1st Tank Destroyer Brigade (not a cavalry group), and it served from 3 August to the fall of Brest (20 September) and was "soon dissolved".

- **Semantic scenario 24 (12 expectations, 368 in all).** It checks the 1st Airborne Task Force under the Seventh Army's direct control on D-day and with no relation after its dissolution, the 1st Special Service Force under the Seventh Army on D-day and with the task force on 26 August, the 517th with the task force at Les Arcs and under the XVIII Airborne Corps and the 82nd Airborne Division on 3 January 1945, the 551st with no relation after its inactivation, Task Force Butler under VI Corps at Sisteron and also under the 36th Division at Condillac, Task Force A under VIII Corps at Morlaix, and the first and last claim ids of the batch.

- **Docs.** `docs/HANDOFF.md` (claim count 1,758, next ids `C1759`, `CR1040`, `PL0907`, `R701`, validator 41 warnings, semantic tests 368) and `docs/roadmap.md` (Brigades Batch 2 and its seven open points). 19 new validator warnings, each explained in its row: 18 "attached entry without a standing parent" for the separate units and `US-SSF-001B` line 6 (the disbandment row asserts the earlier end of the task-force attachment).

## brigades-uk-can-1944-45: Brigades Batch 1, British and Canadian independent brigades (29 October 2026)

Scott approved 35 independent brigades in three batches; this is Batch 1 (rows 1 to 12 of the planning list). Each period and claim was checked against Ellis (Victory in the West), Stacey (The Victory Campaign) and Wikipedia; where the planning list (written from memory) differed from the sources, the sources were followed and the difference is listed below. Command relations follow the batch convention: a corps is `assigned`, a division `attached` (temporary), and a move into the 79th Armoured Division `organic`. Ends not dated by a source are closed at the next claim (basis `inferred_from_next_claim`). One commit per item.

- **Armoured and tank brigades (32 claims, C1658 to C1689).** 4th Armoured (5), 6th Guards Tank (5), 8th Armoured (5), 27th Armoured (4), 31st Tank (2), 33rd Armoured (4) and 34th Tank (7). Four placeholders became active (27th, 33rd, 6th Guards, 34th). 13 new places; two new Wikipedia references (R687, R688). Inferred values, each annotated in the record (old value: none, the row is new; new value; reason):
  - 27th Armoured Brigade, 29 July 1944, break-up: a representative point in the Caen sector, confidence 0.3, because no source places the brigade on the day; the date is Ellis's and the Wikipedia regiment list's (29 July), not the 27 July of the Wikipedia lead.
  - 31st Tank Brigade, 4 September 1944, transfer to the 79th Armoured Division: confidence 0.3, a representative point in the Caen sector because no source places the brigade on the day (its regiments were converting to Crocodiles); the relation to the 79th is `organic` from that date.
  - 34th Tank Brigade, 6 November 1944, Willemstad: confidence 0.3, the objective named by Wikipedia stands for the brigade's position.
  - 34th Tank Brigade, 15 July 1944: the XII Corps relation is inferred from Stacey naming Greenline as the XII Corps operation (p. 169), starting on the row date.
  - 6th Guards Tank Brigade, 27 March 1945: the attachment to XVIII Airborne Corps starts on the row date (Ellis, vol. II, p. 305, gives no date); the few days under the US Ninth Army are not recorded.
  - Division attachments with no stated end are closed at the next claim under a different parent.
- **Corrections to the planning list (armour):** 27th Armoured Brigade broken up on 29 July 1944 (Ellis; the list said 27 July); 31st Tank Brigade arrived on 19 June 1944 and its independent period ends on 4 September 1944, when it joined the 79th Armoured Division; 33rd Armoured Brigade is shown "from 79th Armoured Division" in Ellis's Rhine order of battle, so its independent period ends early in 1945 (open question); 34th Tank Brigade: Wikipedia puts it in the 79th Armoured Division in February 1945 while Ellis lists it as independent (open question); the 6th Guards and 34th brigades were renamed Armoured in 1945 (unit ids kept).
- **Infantry and commando brigades (17 claims, C1690 to C1706).** New units UK-INF-056B (56th Infantry Brigade, re-formed 15 February 1944) and UK-INF-115B (115th Infantry Brigade); the 1st and 4th Special Service Brigades became active. 56th Infantry Brigade (6): the Gold landing, Bayeux, Tilly-sur-Seulles under the 7th Armoured Division, the opening of Bluecoat at Saint-Germain-d'Ectot, the Orne near Thury-Harcourt under the 59th Division and the move into the 49th Division; 115th Infantry Brigade (1); 1st Special Service (Commando) Brigade (4): Ouistreham, Blackcock, Well and Wesel; 4th Special Service (Commando) Brigade (6): Saint-Aubin, Dunkirk, De Haan, Westkapelle, the last Walcheren strong-point and the take-over of Walcheren. Seven new places. The deferred relation D01 (the 56th Infantry Brigade attached to the 50th Division, claim C0017) is now relation CR0997 and has been removed from `docs/deferred_relations.md`; the name is replaced by the unit id in the `composing_units` of C0017 and C0023. One overlap (CR0998 and CR0999, the handover day of 12 June 1944) is documented in `data/command_relation_overlaps.csv`. Inferred values, each annotated in the record (old value: none, the row is new, unless stated):
  - 56th Infantry Brigade, 20 August 1944: a representative point (Caen sector), confidence 0.3; the attachment to the 59th Division and the XII Corps relation closed on 19 August (old value no end, new value 19 August), because the brigade was integral to the 49th Division from 20 August (Ellis).
  - 115th Infantry Brigade, 23 March 1945: a representative point (Xanten) on the XII Corps Rhine front, confidence 0.3; only the order of battle places it.
  - 1st Special Service Brigade, about 20 January 1945 (Blackcock): the day is approximate (no source read gives it), confidence 0.3; the 1944 relations to I Corps and the 6th Airborne Division closed on 8 September 1944 (old value no end, new value 8 September), when the brigade returned to England (Wikipedia).
  - 4th Special Service Brigade, end of November 1944: the date is taken as 30 November (Stacey says "the end of November"), confidence 0.3; the 3rd Canadian Division attachment of D-Day closed on 15 September 1944 (old value no end, new value 15 September), the day before the Dunkirk row.
  - Corps relations of the brigades start on the row dates where the sources do not date them.
- **Corrections to the planning list (infantry and commandos):** the 56th Infantry Brigade was formed on 15 February 1944 and became part of the 49th Division on 20 August 1944 (Ellis, Appendix IV; Wikipedia), so only 6 June to 20 August 1944 is in scope; the 115th Infantry Brigade arrived in North-West Europe on 12 February 1945, not in April (Wikipedia), and only Ellis's Rhine order of battle places it (TracesOfWar puts "the 115th" at Flushing, which was the 155th Brigade of the 52nd Division); the 1st Special Service Brigade returned to England on 8-9 September 1944 and came back for Blackcock in January 1945, renamed the 1st Commando Brigade.
- **2nd Canadian Armoured Brigade (7 claims, C1707 to C1713).** Le Mesnil-Patry (11 June 1944), Colombelles in Atlantic (18 July), Saint-André-sur-Orne under the 2nd Canadian Division (21 July), the Totalize start line (7 August), Calais (25 September), short of Breda under the Polish Armoured Division (27 October) and the Calcar ridge in Blockbuster (26 February 1945); one new place. The brigade's existing D-Day row (C0001) is unchanged. The II Canadian Corps relation starts at 3 p.m. on 11 July 1944, as Stacey states (p. 166). Inferred values, each annotated in the record (old value: none, the row is new, unless stated): the brigade's relation to Second Army from D-Day (CR0002) and the attachment to the 3rd Canadian Division (CR0001) are closed the day before the next row under another higher unit (old value no end, new value 10 June and 20 July 1944); the other corps and division relations start on the row dates where the sources do not date them; the 26 February 1945 attachment to the 2nd Canadian Division stands for two regiments of the brigade.
- **Corrections to the planning list (Canadian):** the Moyland woods action of 19 February 1945 (Ellis, vol. II, p. 268) gives only that regiments of the brigade were to relieve the Guards tank battalions, with no place for the brigade, so the planned row was replaced by the Calcar ridge row of 26 February (Stacey, p. 492).
- **Semantic scenario 23 (12 expectations, 356 in all).** It checks the 56th Infantry Brigade's moves of 11-12 June 1944 and the end of its independent period, the 27th Armoured Brigade with no relation after its break-up, the 1st Special Service Brigade with no relation while in England, the 4th Special Service Brigade at Walcheren, the 2nd Canadian Armoured Brigade under II Canadian Corps and at Breda, the 4th Armoured Brigade at Verden, and the batch's first and last rows. Writing it showed that the 27th Armoured Brigade's two relations still ran on after the break-up: the I Corps relation (CR0977) now ends on 29 July 1944, stated by the break-up row C1676 (old value no end, new value 29 July 1944, reason the break-up, Ellis vol. I p. 522), and the 3rd Division attachment (CR0978) is closed on the same day as an inferred value (end between 18 and 29 July).

## cleanup-2-late-war-west: follow-up after Scott's answers (29 October 2026)

Scott accepted the five recommendations of the audit (`docs/review/cleanup2_audit.md`). One commit per item.

- **Row-by-row read of the 84 rows with two cited families (open question 1): 6 raised from 0.5 to 0.7, 78 unchanged.** A row was raised only where both source families give the day and the place. In each raised row the place is an area, an objective town or an unsurveyed landmark, so the rubric value is 0.7 (approximate place, two families), not 0.9. Old value 0.5, new value 0.7, with the reason in a Review sentence of each record:
  - 82nd Airborne Division, landing at Groesbeek, 17 September 1944 (TracesOfWar and the division's Operation Market historical data).
  - 82nd Airborne Division, command post CHAMPION west of Groesbeek, 17 September 1944 (same two sources).
  - 4th Armored Division, landing at Utah Beach, 11 July 1944 (USHMM and Wikipedia).
  - VI Corps, Dragoon assault, 15 August 1944 (CMH Riviera to the Rhine and Wikipedia).
  - VIII Corps, Avranches entered, 30 July 1944 (CMH Breakout and Pursuit and Wikipedia).
  - 89th Infantry Division, Rhine crossing at St. Goar, 26 March 1945 (CMH Last Offensive and Wikipedia).
  The other 78 were left: in them the second family gives only the unit, the army, the operation dates, a month, or a span, or the place rests on one family, or the date or place is inferred or representative.
- **Four British front-line rows widened to 10 km (open question 2).** Old precision, new precision 10 km; the point Ellis names, the place, the date and the confidence are unchanged; the reason is that each row is a corps or army front, and the sector rule is 10 km. Each record has a Review sentence. Rows: Second Army, 29 August 1944 (Vernon, 5 km); XII Corps, 4 August 1944 (Évrecy, 5 km); XXX Corps, 21 August 1944 (the Touques near Gacé, 3 km) and 17 September 1944 (Valkenswaard, 3 km). Validator warnings fall from 24 to 20.
- **Open questions 3 to 5 accepted with no change:** the 7th Armored Division command post of 13 August 1944 stays as the CMH list gives it; the 231 date gaps are left for a later batch; the 9 open conflict groups wait for a new source. Recorded in the audit report.

## cleanup-2-late-war-west: clean-up of the late-war Western Front data (29 October 2026)

Scott asked for a review and clean-up pass of the Western Front data (6 June 1944 to 8 May 1945, division level and above, all nationalities) using only the sources already in the data. The audit is `docs/review/cleanup2_audit.md`. One commit per item.

- **Duplicate places merged (4).** Four places were entered twice by different batches: Hébécrevon (Manche), Stolberg (Rhineland), Évrecy (Calvados) and Bains-les-Bains (Vosges). In each pair the older row survives; the newer row is retired in `data/place_merges.csv` (the id is not reused) and its name is kept as an alias of the survivor. Seven claims were re-pointed: the old value is the retired place, the new value is the survivor, the reason is that both rows are the same commune; each claim note says so. Coordinates did not change: for Hébécrevon, Stolberg and Bains-les-Bains the two points were identical; the two Évrecy claims of the British batch keep their own Nominatim point (about 100 m from the Wikipedia point of the survivor) with a `coord_override` that says so.
- **Operation date warning cleared (1 claim).** The 7th Armoured Division row of 8 August 1944 (Mont Pinçon, making for Condé-sur-Noireau) is labelled Operation Bluecoat, whose end date (7 August) comes from the Wikipedia infobox, while Ellis describes the Bluecoat fighting to 8 August. No value changed: the row is listed in `data/operation_exceptions.csv` with that reason, which removes one validator warning (25 to 24).
- **Confidence raised (1 claim).** The First Army (German) row of 8 September 1944, its transfer to Army Group G: old 0.5, new 0.7. Reason: the rubric gives 0.7 to an approximate place with two source families; the CMH (Lorraine Campaign p. 193) and the OKW war diary (Schramm p. 370) both give the day and both describe the army sector that the representative point (Metz, 50 km) stands for. The audit found 227 rows at 0.3 or 0.5 that cite references of two families. The notes of the 76 that state two families were read: in every other one the second family covers only the day, only the unit or only the context, gives a month-level date, or the point is a representative headquarters point (0.3 by the rubric), so none was raised. 67 pair the CMH with the World War II by Day compilation of the same CMH command-post lists, which is not a second family, so they stay. The other 84 were not read one by one; they are an open question in the audit.
- **Resolved open questions removed from the documents (Scott asked for it; documents only, no data changed).** Each was checked against a merged change, Scott's answer or the changelog before it was removed; open questions that are still unresolved were kept, and the remaining items keep their numbers. Removed:
  - `docs/roadmap.md`: the 2d French Armored Division's attachment to XV Corps (written by the French batch); XXII and XXIII Corps as placeholders (activated); the 95th Division's April corps and the 100th Division's April parent (both answered by Scott on 27 October 2026); the AT and CZ country codes (approved by Scott); the 243rd Division's destroyed flag (settled by the clean-up of 28 October 2026, in three lists); the Panzer Lehr and 716th Division conflict groups (settled on 28 October 2026; the other three groups stay open); the 353rd Division's corps (settled by note on 28 October 2026); the end of the 7th Armoured and Guards Armoured attachments to II Canadian Corps (closed by British Batch 1, two lists); extending I Corps in the British batch (done); an operation row for Tractable (added) and for Plunder (added); the 2nd Canadian Division's Boxtel reserve row (written by Canadian Batch 2); the Polish, Belgian, Dutch and Czechoslovak formations and the 11th Armoured Division's attachment to the Canadian corps (Allied contingents batch and British Batch 2, two lists); the XVIII Airborne Corps under Second Army for Plunder (British Batch 2 follow-up); the 43rd Division "Mechelen" row (written as Megchelen); a second British batch (done); the Astonia, Infatuate, Pheasant and Clipper operations (added).
  - `docs/HANDOFF.md`: merging and deleting the handoff and October open-items branches (only `main` exists on GitHub); the list of 27 unfilled US placeholders and the question whether other nations get rows (all done, each batch approved by Scott); the quarterly batch split (both batches approved and merged); where the "Model to 14 September" date came from (settled by the German Batch 2 follow-up: the data never had it).
  - `docs/review/verification_results.md`: the four relations "waiting for Scott" and the list of "new decisions pending" (all decided and applied on 26 October 2026; the decisions themselves stay recorded in the items above them).
  - Kept because still open: the corps that the CMH table entries "X" and "XIV" meant, XXII and XXIII Corps in April and May, the three open German conflict groups, the 243rd Division dissolution row (not approved), combat commands, the commanders table, the outside-review questions, the licence, the 43rd Division's move to VIII Corps on 2 September 1944, and the other open points with their recommendations.
## rule-copyrighted-notes: notes from copyrighted sources stay private (29 October 2026)

Docs and rules only, from `main` (`941b8b0`), with `main` (`d99fc07`) merged in; no data changed. Scott agreed that notes or extracts from copyrighted sources are kept only on the owner's working computer and in his Google Drive (`WWII Unit Locations` folder) and are never committed to the remote repository.

- **`docs/HANDOFF.md`**, section 3: new standing rule 16.
- **`CONTRIBUTING.md`**, Rules: new rule 10.
- **`.gitignore`**: new entries `**/sources/*_notes.md`, `docs/sources/` and `private_notes/`. No tracked file matches them (checked with `git ls-files` and `git check-ignore`).
- No script changed: the sync tool replays only committed files, so ignored files cannot reach the publishing clone.

## french-followup-1944-45: de Lattre pass (29 October 2026)

Same unmerged branch `french-followup-1944-45` (head before the pass `a7113df`; `main` `941b8b0` unchanged). Scott approved using Jean de Lattre de Tassigny, *The History of the French First Army* (trans. Malcolm Barnes, Allen & Unwin 1952; archive.org `bwb_W7-ADK-972`, borrow-only). The book was not re-opened: the evidence is the research notes taken from its OCR text layer (about 335 lines, printed pp. 63-525, gap at p. ~263), each line's quote and printed page. A fact only in a note's paraphrase columns, or only in a truncated or garbled quote, is not used on its own; OCR corrections are stated in the row (`ist` = `1st` in CR0959).

### References and documents

- **`R686`** (new): de Lattre, *The History of the French First Army* (1952); `source_type` `unit_history`, `source_family` `delattre_french_first_army` (its own family, independent of CMH and Wikipedia). Now cited by 6 claims.
- **Research notes**: the notes taken from the book (with a header giving citation, method and known gaps) are kept privately by the project owner (Drive, WWII Unit Locations folder), not in this repository; they were briefly committed on this branch and removed in a later commit. `docs/sources.md`: one row for the source.

### Claims

- **`C1644`** (9th DIC, Toulon, 27 Aug 1944): confidence 0.5 to 0.7. de Lattre pp. 76, 92-93 (St Catherine fort 24 August; Ruhfus's surrender 27 August 23.45) is a second family for the city and day; the quotes do not name the division, the note line does.
- **`C1633`** (5th DB, Stuttgart, 21 Apr 1945): confidence 0.3 to 0.5. de Lattre pp. 460-466: "when Stuttgart fell on the 21st April"; the link of the day to the division rests on the note's summary, so not 0.7.
- **`C1634`** (1st DFL, Lyon, 3 Sep 1944): de Lattre p. 128 (08.00 on 3 September) added as a third family; stays 0.7 (city, 10 km).
- **`C1637`** (2nd DIM, Belfort, 25 Nov 1944): de Lattre pp. 243-246 has Belfort fallen by 21 November; Review note, date and 0.7 unchanged (not reconciled).
- **`C1656`** (new, 3rd DIA, Strasbourg, 5 Jan 1945, 0.5): de Lattre pp. 309, 312 and CMH *Riviera to the Rhine* pp. 517, 522, 527.
- **`C1657`** (new, 1st DB, Ulm, 23 Apr 1945, 0.3): CMH *The Last Offensive* p. 431 (French platoon outside Ulm at dusk) and de Lattre pp. 482, 485-486; attribution to the division inferred. New place `PL0851` Ulm (OpenStreetMap).

### Relations

- **`CR0942`** (2DB under the Seventh Army) and **`CR0953`** (2DB attached to the XV Corps): old valid_to 1945-01-27 (inferred); new 1945-01-18 (narrative): de Lattre p. 373, the combat commands Dio and Langlade left the US Seventh Army on 19 January (two of three; the third is not dated). `C1647` now `asserts_end` for both.
- **`CR0938`** (2DB under the First French Army): old valid_from 1945-01-28 (claim date); new 1945-01-19 (narrative, de Lattre p. 373; `C1648` `asserts_start`).
- **`CR0958`** (new): 2DB under the French II Corps from 28 January 1945 (claim date; CMH p. 548 "leaving Leclerc's 2d Armored Division with de Monsabert"; de Lattre pp. 352, 369-371); end unknown, earliest 8 February. No XXI Corps relation was added: the note for p. 394 puts the XXI Corps link only in its paraphrase and the CMH history leaves the division with the II Corps.
- **`CR0959`** (new): 14th DI under the I Corps from 23 April 1945 (de Lattre p. 479, OCR `ist` read as `1st`; p. 459), asserted by `C1655`.
- **`CR0960`** (new): 3rd DIA under the II Corps from 5 January 1945 (de Lattre p. 312); end inferred (day before the Speyer row), unknown.
- Notes only: `CR0951`, `CR0952` confirmed (de Lattre pp. 286, 287, 302); `CR0946` confirmed (p. 123); `CR0947` kept at 1 September, with de Lattre's "officially went into action at midnight of the 5th to 6th September" (p. 134) recorded; `CR0956` consistent (p. 406).

### Not done (and why)

- 1st DFL to the Alps army detachment from 1 March 1945 (p. 405-406): no 1st DFL row after January 1945 and no place in the quote, so no relation can be anchored; open point.
- I Corps activation (5/6 Sep) and II Corps (1 Sep) as claims: no place in the quotes; recorded on `CR0947`/`CR0946` instead.
- 5th DB at Bregenz (p. 498), 4th DMM at Rouffach (pp. 388-390), 9th DIC at Ensisheim (p. 396), 2nd DB near Obernai-Marlenheim in January (p. 331): the quote does not name the place or the unit, or is truncated.

### Scripts

- **`scripts/semantic_tests.py`:** scenario 21 updated (2DB on 1 Feb 1945 now also under the II Corps; C1633 now 0.5); new scenario 22, de Lattre pass: 7 expectations. Total: 344 expectations hold. No other script changed.

## french-followup-1944-45: French follow-up refinements (29 October 2026)

New branch `french-followup-1944-45` from `main` (`941b8b0`, after PR #32 merged the French batch). Scott on 29 October 2026 accepted the seven recommendations of the French batch and asked for a follow-up. Sources: the CMH histories (*Riviera to the Rhine*, *The Last Offensive*), English Wikipedia and, new, French Wikipedia (`R682` to `R684`, same source family as English Wikipedia). De Lattre's *History of the French First Army* (1952) was not read in this first pass (the archive.org copies are borrow-only); it was used later from research notes, see the de Lattre pass above.

### Claims and relations

- **2d French Armored Division, December 1944 to January 1945 (corrected):**
  - The VI Corps relation `CR0937` ran from 27 November 1944 to 7 February 1945 (end inferred from the next row). It now ends on 4 December 1944: the 6th Army Group transferred operational control of the division from the Seventh to the First French Army effective 5 December (*Riviera to the Rhine*, p. 464). Old: valid_to 1945-02-07, inferred, earliest 1944-11-28. New: valid_to 1944-12-04, narrative. The link to `C1616` now asserts both start and end.
  - The Seventh Army relation `CR0942` (standing, unspecified_subordination) ended on 7 February 1945 (inferred). It now ends on 27 January 1945, inferred from the new Grussenheim row (earliest 3 January). It is kept through December because the Seventh Army continued the division's logistical support while the First French Army held operational control (p. 464).
  - The First French Army relation `CR0938` started on 8 February 1945 (the Fessenheim row). It now starts on 28 January 1945, the new Grussenheim row (claim_date); earliest end now 8 February.
  - New `CR0951`: First French Army, operational_control, 5 to 27 December 1944 (p. 464; ended by 6th Army Group Letter of Instructions 7 of 28 December, p. 496, note 7).
  - New `CR0952`: French II Corps, attached, 15 (claim_date) to 27 December 1944 (p. 486).
  - New `CR0953`: XV Corps, attached, 3 January (claim_date) to 27 January 1945 (end inferred from the next row): the division moved to the Sarre valley as XV Corps reserve (p. 501) and supported its counterattacks (p. 505).
  - 3 new claims, `C1646` to `C1648`: the push south from the Selestat region (15 December 1944, representative point, 0.3), the XV Corps sector near Rimling (3 January 1945, date approximate, representative point, 0.3) and Grussenheim (28 January 1945, 0.5).
- **New units (3) and promoted placeholders (2):**
  - `FR-ARMY-ATLANT`, French Forces of the West (Détachement d'Armée de l'Atlantique from March 1945): 1 claim, `C1649`, creation at Cognac on 14 October 1944 (0.5). The two French Wikipedia articles give 1 March and 25 March 1945 for the renaming; noted in the unit row, not reconciled.
  - `FR-INF-GIRONDE`, Gironde marching division (General d'Anselme): 1 claim, `C1650`, operation Vénérable against Royan, 15 April 1945 (0.5). `CR0954`: under the Atlantic detachment, unspecified_subordination, from 15 April (claim_date; the subordination is inferred from the single command of the operation).
  - `FR-INF-027D`, 27th Alpine Infantry Division: 1 claim, `C1651`, the offensive toward the Little St Bernard Pass, 23 March 1945 (representative point, 0.3). `CR0957`: 6th Army Group, unspecified_subordination, from 23 March (claim_date), through General Doyen's Alpine army detachment, which is not a unit.
  - `FR-INF-010D` (placeholder promoted): 2 claims, `C1652` and `C1653`: reconstituted in the Nevers region on 1 October 1944 (0.3) and policing the interior of the Colmar Pocket from 5 February 1945 (CMH p. 551, representative point Colmar, 0.3). `CR0955`: First French Army from 5 February (claim_date).
  - `FR-INF-014D` (placeholder promoted): 2 claims, `C1654` and `C1655`: recreated with its headquarters at Buhl on 9 February 1945 (0.3) and on Lake Constance at the end of the war (8 May 1945, date inferred, 0.3). `CR0956`: First French Army from 9 February (claim_date).
  - Not added: a 1st Alpine Division (no source found), and a division-level La Rochelle force (the sources read describe only a sector under Colonel Chêne and the Oléron brigade).
- **Karlsruhe (`C1639`):** already kept at 3 April 1945 with the CMH 4 April noted; not changed.
- **5th Armored Division at Stuttgart (`C1633`):** date confirmed, 21 April 1945: *The Last Offensive*, p. 429, says the boundary changed on the 20th and a French column broke into Stuttgart the next day; the city was clear on 22 April. The division is not named there, so confidence stays 0.3. Quotes and notes refined; date unchanged.
- **Confidence raised (old 0.5, new 0.7), each annotated in the row:** `C1634` 1st DFL at Lyon, 3 September 1944 (*Riviera to the Rhine*, p. 181); `C1637` 2nd Moroccan Division at Belfort, 25 November 1944 (p. 419); `C1645` 9th Colonial Division at Ensisheim, 6 February 1945 (p. 551; the CMH names the division among the three of the I Corps main effort, so treated as approximate). The other French rows at 0.3 and 0.5 were checked against the CMH chapters; no second source family names the unit at the place, so they are unchanged.
- **Places:** 6 new, `PL0845` to `PL0850`.
- **Claim totals:** 10 new (France), 3 at 0.5 and 7 at 0.3; 4 existing rows changed (`C1633`, `C1634`, `C1637`, `C1645`).
- **Side effect:** the reference tool filled the blank read date (2026-10-27) on `R028` (14th Infantry Division, Wikipedia), as in the French batch.

### Scripts (flag for Scott)

- **`scripts/semantic_tests.py`:** new scenario 21, French follow-up: 8 expectations. Total: 337 expectations hold.
- **`validate.py` and the build:** not changed.

### Validator warnings

There are 25 warnings, the same as before. None is on a French row.

## french-1944-45: the French formations (29 October 2026)

New branch `french-1944-45` from `main` (`b021dc2`). It covers the Western Front, 6 June 1944 to 8 May 1945, division level and above; Italy is not covered. Sources: the CMH histories (Blumenson, *Breakout and Pursuit*; Cole, *The Lorraine Campaign*; Clarke and Smith, *Riviera to the Rhine*; MacDonald, *The Last Offensive*) and the Wikipedia unit and battle articles. Five references are new: `R677` (Riviera to the Rhine ch. 30), `R678` (ch. 2), `R679` (ch. 12), `R680` (Wikipedia, 1st Armored Division (France)) and `R681` (Wikipedia, 5th Armored Division (France)). The eleven units were placeholders and are promoted by `promote_placeholders.py`. The nationality folder `data/claims/france` is new. De Lattre's history was not consulted in this pass and is not cited.

### Claims and relations

- **2d French Armored Division (Leclerc):**
